          node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js
          node builder-control/test/dashboard-slice.test.cjs

      - name: Engineering OS — compute the subject once
        # ONE computation of subject paths and subject hash, reused by every
        # step below. Computing it twice invites two answers, and the whole
        # value of a bound review is that there is exactly one thing it is
        # bound to.
        id: subject
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
            # CONFIRMED FINDING #2: this used `--subject` with no refs, which
            # runs `git diff HEAD` — EMPTY on a clean Actions checkout. The
            # empty subject was then classified LIGHT and skipped the binding
            # gate entirely, so the fallback silently disabled enforcement on
            # exactly the runs that needed it. HEAD's own file list is now
            # enumerated explicitly, and an empty result fails closed below.
            echo "Base '$BASE' unavailable — enumerating HEAD's own files."
            USED_BASE=""
            HEADFILES=$(git diff-tree --root --no-commit-id -r --name-only HEAD | sed '/^$/d')
            if [ -z "$HEADFILES" ]; then
              echo "BLOCKED: no trustworthy file range could be established for HEAD."
              echo "An unknown subject is never treated as a small one."
              exit 1
            fi
            CHANGEDARGS=()
            while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && CHANGEDARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done <<< "$HEADFILES"
            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject "${CHANGEDARGS[@]}" --json)
          else
            USED_BASE="$BASE"
            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject --base "$BASE" --head HEAD --json)
          fi
          echo "$SUBJ"
          SHA=$(echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).subjectSha256))')
          echo "sha=$SHA" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>{const j=JSON.parse(s);console.log(j.subjectPaths.join("\n"))})' > /tmp/subject-paths.txt
          echo "count=$(wc -l < /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr -d " ")" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          # Record the EXACT base used, so the gate step certifies the same
          # range rather than recomputing a different one.
          echo "base=$USED_BASE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

      - name: Engineering OS — bind to exactly one task packet
        # A change that carries two modified packets has two competing
        # authorizations and no deterministic way to choose between them.
        # Rather than pick one, this blocks and says so.
        id: packet
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
            CHANGED=$(git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r HEAD)
          else
            CHANGED=$(git diff --name-only "$BASE" HEAD)
          fi
          PACKETS=$(echo "$CHANGED" | grep -E '^builder-control/packets/.*\.json$' || true)
          COUNT=$(if [ -z "$PACKETS" ]; then echo 0; else echo "$PACKETS" | wc -l | tr -d ' '; fi)
          echo "changed packets: $COUNT"
          echo "$PACKETS"
          if [ "$COUNT" -gt 1 ]; then
            echo ''
            echo 'BLOCKED: this change modifies more than one task packet.'
            echo 'Exactly one packet authorizes a change. Two packets means two'
            echo 'answers to "what was this allowed to touch", and there is no'
            echo 'honest way to pick. Split the change.'
            exit 1
          fi
          if [ "$COUNT" -eq 1 ]; then
            echo "active=$PACKETS" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "── pinning intent for $PACKETS"
            node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --spec-check --packet "$PACKETS"
          else
            echo "active=" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            echo "No task packet added or modified by this change."
          fi

      - name: Engineering OS — classify the subject
        id: classify
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          SUBJECT_BASE="${{ steps.subject.outputs.base }}"
          ARGS=()
          while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && ARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done < /tmp/subject-paths.txt
          if [ ${#ARGS[@]} -eq 0 ]; then
            # FINDING #2: an empty subject used to be declared LIGHT, which is
            # how a computation failure became a governance bypass. Zero subject
            # paths after a successful subject computation means the change is
            # evidence-only; that is reported, and the lane is left UNKNOWN so
            # the gate step still runs and decides.
            echo "No subject paths — evidence-only change. Lane left UNKNOWN; the gate still runs."
            echo "lane=UNKNOWN" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
            exit 0
          fi
          # The classifier now refuses LIGHT without a line count (finding #10),
          # so CI must supply the real one rather than let an unknown size pass.
          if [ -n "$SUBJECT_BASE" ]; then
            LINES=$(git diff --numstat "$SUBJECT_BASE" HEAD -- $(cat /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr '\n' ' ') | awk '{a+=$1+$2} END {print a+0}')
          else
            LINES=$(git diff --numstat HEAD -- $(cat /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr '\n' ' ') | awk '{a+=$1+$2} END {print a+0}')
          fi
          echo "subject changed lines: $LINES"
          node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --classify "${ARGS[@]}" --diff-lines "$LINES" | tee /tmp/classify.txt
          LANE=$(awk -F': *' '/^lane /{print $2; exit}' /tmp/classify.txt | awk '{print $1}')
          echo "lane=$LANE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          {
            echo "### Engineering OS — change classification"
            echo ''
            echo '```'
            cat /tmp/classify.txt
            echo '```'
          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

      - name: Engineering OS — required review evidence (binding)
        # Fail-closed. Missing review evidence blocks the merge. A review bound
        # to a different subject hash does not count. An unavailable reviewer
        # blocks and is reported as UNAVAILABLE rather than upgraded to a pass.
        #
        # --subject-sha is passed explicitly and is mandatory in the CLI: a gate
        # decision with no bound subject certifies nothing at all.
        # Runs for FULL and for UNKNOWN. Only a positively-classified LIGHT skips.
        if: steps.classify.outputs.lane != 'LIGHT'
        run: |
          set -euo pipefail
          ARGS=()
          while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && ARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done < /tmp/subject-paths.txt
          SUBJECT_BASE="${{ steps.subject.outputs.base }}"
          PACKET="${{ steps.packet.outputs.active }}"
          PKTARG=()
          if [ -n "$PACKET" ]; then PKTARG=(--packet "$PACKET"); fi
          # CONFIRMED FINDING (#2): this previously passed only --changed. The
          # subject above was computed over BASE..HEAD, but --gate-done with no
          # refs recomputes over `git diff HEAD` — a different range, therefore
          # a different hash, therefore ENGOS-SUBJECT-MISMATCH on every real
          # pull request even when the reviews were correct. The gate must be
          # handed the exact same range it is being asked to certify.
          REFARGS=()
          if [ -n "${SUBJECT_BASE:-}" ]; then REFARGS=(--base "$SUBJECT_BASE" --head HEAD); fi
          node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --gate-done \
            --subject-sha "${{ steps.subject.outputs.sha }}" \
            "${REFARGS[@]+"${REFARGS[@]}"}" \
            "${PKTARG[@]+"${PKTARG[@]}"}" "${ARGS[@]}"
   150	//      in the subject diff, writing one would change the hash, which would
   151	//      invalidate the record that was just written — no set of reviews could
   152	//      ever satisfy the gate. Excluding them makes adding evidence a no-op on
   153	//      the subject hash, which is what lets the loop terminate at all.
   154	//
   155	//   2. Control metadata must not escalate risk. builder-control/ is high-risk
   156	//      because the control CODE is high-risk. A perfectly ordinary front-end PR
   157	//      that happens to carry its own packet and review records would otherwise
   158	//      inherit "the control system itself" and be forced to HIGH forever.
   159	//
   160	// Control CODE (gate.cjs, engineering-os.cjs, the doctrine) stays in the
   161	// subject and stays high-risk. Only evidence artifacts are excluded.
   162	const EVIDENCE_EXCLUDE = [
   163	  'builder-control/reviews/**',
   164	  'builder-control/review-raw/**',
   165	  'builder-control/packets/**',
   166	  'builder-control/ledger.json',
   167	];
   168	
   169	function isEvidencePath(p) {
   170	  return EVIDENCE_EXCLUDE.some((g) => globToRe(g).test(p));
   171	}
   172	
   173	// Compute subject paths + the hash reviewers bind to.
   174	// changedPaths may be supplied explicitly; otherwise it is derived from git.
   175	function computeSubject({ base, head, changed }) {
   176	  let changedPaths;
   177	  if (Array.isArray(changed) && changed.length) {
   178	    changedPaths = changed.slice();
   179	  } else {
   180	    const h = assertRef(head || 'HEAD', '--head');
   181	    if (base) {
   182	      const b = assertRef(base, '--base');
   183	      changedPaths = git(['diff', '--name-only', `${b}..${h}`]).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   184	    } else {
   185	      changedPaths = git(['diff', '--name-only', h]).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   186	    }
   187	  }
   188	  changedPaths = [...new Set(changedPaths)].sort();
   189	
   190	  const subjectPaths = changedPaths.filter((p) => !isEvidencePath(p));
   191	  const excluded = changedPaths.filter(isEvidencePath);
   192	
   193	  // Hash ONLY the subject paths. `--` separates paths from refs so a path that
   194	  // looks like a ref cannot be reinterpreted as one.
   195	  let diff = '';
   196	  if (subjectPaths.length) {
   197	    const h = assertRef(head || 'HEAD', '--head');
   198	    const args = ['diff'];
   199	    if (base) args.push(`${assertRef(base, '--base')}..${h}`);
   200	    else args.push(h);
   201	    args.push('--', ...subjectPaths);
   202	    diff = git(args);
   203	  }
   204	
   205	  return {
   206	    changedPaths,
   207	    subjectPaths,
   208	    excludedAsEvidence: excluded,
   209	    subjectSha256: sha256(diff),
   210	    diffBytes: diff.length,
   211	    range: base ? `${base}..${head || 'HEAD'}` : (head || 'HEAD'),
   212	  };
   213	}
   214	
   215	function parseArgs(argv) {
   216	  const a = { changed: [], review: [], _: [] };
   217	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   218	    const t = argv[i];
   219	    if (t === '--changed') a.changed.push(argv[++i]);
   220	    else if (t === '--review') a.review.push(argv[++i]);
   221	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   222	    else if (t === '--diff-lines') a.diffLines = Number(argv[++i]);
   223	    else if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   224	    else if (t === '--diff-sha') a.diffSha = argv[++i];
   225	    else if (t === '--run-checks') a.runChecks = true;
   226	    else if (t === '--base') a.base = argv[++i];
   227	    else if (t === '--head') a.head = argv[++i];
   228	    else if (t === '--milestone') a.milestone = true;
   229	    else if (t === '--novel') a.novel = true;
   230	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   231	    else if (t === '--ledger') a.ledger = true;
   232	    else if (t.startsWith('--')) a[t.slice(2)] = true;
   233	    else a._.push(t);
   234	  }
   235	  return a;
   236	}
   237	
   238	// Collect every protected path from the existing policy file. We read the
   239	// canonical file rather than restating any path here — a second copy of the
   240	// protected list is precisely the duplicate authority the contract forbids.
   241	// Load the protected-path policy. FAIL CLOSED: a missing or unreadable policy
   242	// file is a hard error, never an empty allow-list. Treating "no policy found"
   243	// as "nothing is protected" would mean a deleted or corrupted policy silently
   244	// unprotects the entire repository — the file that exists to say what must not
   245	// be touched would, by vanishing, permit touching everything.
   246	function protectedGlobs() {
   247	  if (!fs.existsSync(PROTECTED_PATHS)) {
   248	    throw new PolicyError(
   249	      `protected-path policy not found at ${path.relative(ROOT, PROTECTED_PATHS)}. ` +
   250	      'Refusing to classify anything: an absent policy is not an empty policy.'
   251	    );
   252	  }
   253	  let pol;
   254	  try {
   255	    pol = readJSON(PROTECTED_PATHS);
   840	    superseded.add(rec.supersedes);
   841	  }
   842	  const active = bound.filter((r) => !superseded.has(r.reviewId));
   843	  if (superseded.size) observed.push(`${superseded.size} record(s) superseded by an explicit supersedes declaration`);
   844	
   845	  const byReviewer = {};
   846	  for (const rec of active) (byReviewer[rec.reviewer] ||= []).push(rec);
   847	  for (const [who, recs] of Object.entries(byReviewer)) {
   848	    if (recs.length > 1) {
   849	      problems.push({
   850	        rule: 'ENGOS-AMBIGUOUS-REVIEWS',
   851	        detail: `${recs.length} active records from "${who}" for this subject (${recs.map((r) => `${r.reviewId}:${r.disposition}`).join(', ')}). Exactly one verdict per reviewer per subject. Resolve by adding "supersedes" to the record that replaces the other.`,
   852	      });
   853	    }
   854	  }
   855	
   856	  // 7. Packet binding of records.
   857	  if (packetId) {
   858	    for (const rec of active) {
   859	      if (rec.packetId !== packetId) {
   860	        problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-WRONG-PACKET', detail: `${rec.reviewId} is bound to packet ${rec.packetId}, not ${packetId}` });
   861	      }
   862	    }
   863	  }
   864	
   865	  // 8. Coverage: a record must have read the WHOLE subject. Subset coverage is
   866	  //    the quiet failure — a reviewer that saw 3 of 11 files reporting APPROVE
   867	  //    looks identical to one that saw everything.
   868	  const subjectSet = new Set(subject.subjectPaths);
   869	  for (const rec of active) {
   870	    if (rec.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE') continue;
   871	    const seen = new Set((rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.changedPaths) || []);
   872	    const missing = [...subjectSet].filter((p) => !seen.has(p));
   873	    const extra = [...seen].filter((p) => !subjectSet.has(p));
   874	    if (missing.length) {
   875	      problems.push({
   876	        rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-COVERAGE-SHORT',
   877	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) did not cover ${missing.length} subject path(s): ${missing.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}${missing.length > 5 ? ' …' : ''}. Partial coverage is not approval of the whole change.`,
   878	      });
   879	    }
   880	    if (extra.length) {
   881	      problems.push({
   882	        rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-COVERAGE-EXTRA',
   883	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) claims to have reviewed ${extra.length} path(s) that are not in the subject: ${extra.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}. The record does not describe this change.`,
   884	      });
   885	    }
   886	  }
   887	
   888	  // 9. Every required reviewer must have an APPROVE on this exact subject.
   889	  if (byReviewer['claude-self']) {
   890	    observed.push('claude-self record present (recorded for traceability; it satisfies no required slot)');
   891	  }
   892	  for (const need of cls.requiredReviewers) {
   893	    const recs = byReviewer[need] || [];
   894	    if (!recs.length) {
   895	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING', detail: `${need} is required for lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? '/high-risk' : ''} and has no valid review record bound to this subject. Missing evidence is a BLOCK, never an implicit pass.` });
   896	      continue;
   897	    }
   898	    const unavailable = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE');
   899	    if (unavailable) {
   900	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE', detail: `${need} reported UNAVAILABLE: ${unavailable.unavailableReason}. Reported honestly — and it still blocks, because this lane requires that review.` });
   901	      continue;
   902	    }
   903	    const rejected = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'REJECT');
   904	    if (rejected) { problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED', detail: `${need} returned REJECT (${rejected.reviewId})` }); continue; }
   905	    observed.push(`${need}: ${recs.map((r) => r.disposition).join(', ')}`);
   906	  }
   907	
   908	  // 10. Blocking findings — from ACTIVE, SUBJECT-BOUND records only.
   909	  const blocking = [];
   910	  for (const rec of active) {
   911	    for (const f of rec.findings || []) {
   912	      if ((f.severity === 'CRITICAL' || f.severity === 'HIGH') && f.status === 'OPEN') {
   913	        blocking.push({ reviewer: rec.reviewer, severity: f.severity, file: f.file || '(unspecified)', problem: f.problem });
   914	      }
   915	      // A FIXED finding must name a re-verifying record that is itself active
   916	      // and bound to this subject, and that reviewer may not be the builder.
   917	      if (f.status === 'FIXED') {
   918	        const v = active.find((r) => r.reviewId === f.verifiedByReviewId);
   919	        if (!v) {
   920	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-UNVERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED cites verifiedByReviewId "${f.verifiedByReviewId}", which is not an active review record for this subject.` });
   921	        } else if (v.reviewer === 'claude-self') {
   922	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-SELF-VERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED is verified only by claude-self (${v.reviewId}). The builder cannot verify its own fix.` });
   923	        }
   924	      }
   925	    }
   926	  }
   927	  for (const b of blocking) {
   928	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-OPEN-BLOCKING-FINDING', detail: `${b.severity} from ${b.reviewer} in ${b.file}: ${b.problem}` });
   929	  }
   930	
   931	  // 11. Unverified items surfaced, never dropped.
   932	  const unverified = [];
   933	  for (const rec of active) for (const u of rec.unverified || []) unverified.push(`${rec.reviewer}: ${u}`);
   934	  if (typeof specRows !== 'undefined' && specRows) for (const r of specRows.unverified) unverified.push(`spec: ${r.source}`);
   935	
   936	  // 12. Deterministic checks. READY_FOR_PR is only reachable when this process
   937	  //     actually executed them — not when a caller asserts they passed.
   938	  let checks = null;
   939	  if (args.runChecks) {
   940	    if (!args.packet) {
   941	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-CHECKS-NO-PACKET', detail: '--run-checks needs a packet to read testsRequired from' });
   942	    } else {
   943	      checks = runDeterministicChecks(args.packet);
   944	      for (const c of checks) if (c.exit !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED', detail: `${c.cmd} exited ${c.exit}` });
   945	      observed.push(`deterministic checks executed here: ${checks.filter((c) => c.exit === 0).length}/${checks.length} passed`);
   946	    }
   947	  }
   948	
   949	  const ok = problems.length === 0;
   950	  const state = !ok ? 'BLOCKED' : (checks ? STATE_READY_PR : STATE_READY_DET);
   951	
   952	  return { ok, state, problems, observed, unverified, classification: cls, subject, reviewsBound: bound.length, reviewsActive: active.length, reviewsForeign: foreign.length, checks };
   953	}
   954	
   955	// Execute the packet's declared testsRequired. These are command strings from a
   956	// packet that has already passed packet-tools validation and the Builder
   957	// Control gate; they are run through `bash -lc` because that is what they are —
   958	// commands. No caller-supplied data is interpolated into them.
   959	function runDeterministicChecks(packetPath) {
   960	  let pkt;
   961	  try { pkt = readJSON(packetPath); } catch { return []; }
   962	  const cmds = (pkt.testsRequired || []).filter((c) => !/--gate-done/.test(c));
   963	  const results = [];
   964	  for (const cmd of cmds) {
   965	    const r = spawnSync('bash', ['-lc', cmd], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   966	    results.push({ cmd, exit: r.status === null ? 1 : r.status, tail: ((r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr || '')).trim().split('\n').slice(-3).join('\n') });
   967	  }
   968	  return results;
   969	}
   970	
   971	function cmdGateDone(args) {
   972	  const r = gateDone(args);
   973	  if (args.json) { out(JSON.stringify(r, null, 2)); return r.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_BLOCK; }
   974	
   975	  out('ENGINEERING OS — GATE');
   976	  out('='.repeat(52));
   977	  out(`lane            : ${r.classification.lane}${r.classification.highRisk ? ' (HIGH-RISK)' : ''}`);
   978	  out(`subject         : ${r.subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}… (${r.subject.subjectPaths.length} path(s))`);
   979	  out(`required        : ${r.classification.requiredReviewers.join(' + ') || 'none — deterministic checks only'}`);
   980	  out(`review records  : ${r.reviewsActive} active / ${r.reviewsBound} bound / ${r.reviewsForeign} ignored (other subject)`);
   981	  out('');
   982	  out('OBSERVED:');
   983	  for (const o of r.observed) out(`  - ${o}`);
   984	  if (r.checks) {
   985	    out('');
   986	    out('DETERMINISTIC CHECKS (executed by this process):');
   987	    for (const c of r.checks) out(`  exit ${String(c.exit).padEnd(3)} ${c.cmd}`);
   988	  }
   989	  if (r.unverified.length) {
   990	    out('');
   991	    out('UNVERIFIED (not failures — things nobody actually checked):');
   992	    for (const u of r.unverified) out(`  - ${u}`);
   993	  }
   994	  out('');
   995	  if (!r.ok) {
   996	    out('RESULT: BLOCKED');
   997	    out('');
   998	    for (const p of r.problems) { out(`  rule   : ${p.rule}`); out(`  reason : ${p.detail}`); out(''); }
   999	    out('No override flag exists here by design. Resolve the rule, or amend the');
  1000	    out('packet authorization — which itself leaves a ledger trail.');
  1001	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
  1002	  }
  1003	  out(`RESULT: ${r.state}`);
  1004	  out('');
  1005	  if (r.state === STATE_READY_DET) {
  1006	    out('Required review evidence is present and bound to this subject.');
  1007	    out('Deterministic checks were NOT run by this process — run them, or use');
  1008	    out('--run-checks, before treating this as ready for a pull request.');
  1009	  } else {
  1010	    out('Required review evidence is present and bound to this subject, and the');
  1011	    out("packet's deterministic checks were executed here and passed.");
  1012	  }
  1013	  out('');
  1014	  out('This is NOT a claim that the software is correct, and NOT a runtime or');
  1015	  out('deploy verdict. Runtime validation is a separate step that has not run.');
  1016	  return EXIT_PASS;
  1017	}
  1018	// ── ledger append (reuses the ONE ledger) ───────────────────────────────────
  1019	function appendLedger(entry) {
  1020	  const tmp = path.join(require('os').tmpdir(), `engos-ledger-${process.pid}.json`);
  1021	  fs.writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(entry, null, 2));
  1022	  const r = spawnSync('node', [LEDGER_WRITER, '--append', tmp], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
  1023	  try { fs.unlinkSync(tmp); } catch { /* best effort */ }
  1024	  return r;
  1025	}
  1026	
  1027	// ── COMMAND: --start ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1028	// The one command to run when picking up a task. It answers the three questions
  1029	// you actually have at the start — what am I changing, how much review does it
  1030	// need, and what exactly do I type next — and prints the next commands already
  1031	// filled in with this change's real subject hash. Nothing to look up, nothing
  1032	// to remember, no prompt to paste.
  1033	function cmdStart(args) {
  1034	  const subject = computeSubject({ base: args.base, head: args.head, changed: args.changed });
  1035	  const cls = classify(subject.subjectPaths, { milestone: args.milestone, novel: args.novel, diffLines: args.diffLines });
  1036	  const sha = subject.subjectSha256;
  1037	  const pkt = args.packet || '<your-packet>.json';
  1038	  const refs = (args.base ? ` --base ${args.base}` : '') + (args.head ? ` --head ${args.head}` : '');
  1039	
  1040	  if (args.json) { out(JSON.stringify({ subject, classification: cls }, null, 2)); return EXIT_PASS; }
  1041	
  1042	  out('ENGINEERING OS — START');
  1043	  out('='.repeat(60));
  1044	  out(`subject     : ${sha}`);
  1045	  out(`paths       : ${subject.subjectPaths.length}`);
  1046	  for (const p of subject.subjectPaths.slice(0, 12)) out(`              ${p}`);
  1047	  if (subject.subjectPaths.length > 12) out(`              … ${subject.subjectPaths.length - 12} more`);
  1048	  if (subject.excludedAsEvidence.length) out(`evidence    : ${subject.excludedAsEvidence.length} path(s) excluded from the subject`);
  1049	  out('');
  1050	  out(`lane        : ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? ' (HIGH-RISK)' : ''}`);
  1051	  out(`packet      : ${cls.requiresPacket ? 'REQUIRED' : 'not required'}`);
  1052	  out(`reviewers   : ${cls.requiredReviewers.join(' + ') || 'none required'}`);
  1053	  out('');
  1054	  out('NEXT:');
  1055	  if (cls.lane === 'LIGHT') {
  1056	    out('  This is the light lane. Run your deterministic checks and open the PR.');
  1057	    out('  No packet and no model review are required for a change this small.');
  1058	    out('');
  1059	    out(`  node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --gate-done --subject-sha ${sha}${refs}`);
  1060	  } else {
  1061	    let step = 1;
  1062	    if (!args.packet) {
  1063	      out(`  ${step++}. Create a task packet naming this work, then re-run --start --packet <p>.`);
  1064	      out('     node builder-control/packet-tools.cjs --new --agent claude-code --objective "…"');
  1065	    }
  1066	    for (const r of cls.requiredReviewers) {
  1067	      out(`  ${step++}. Run the ${r} review (read-only, bound to this subject):`);
  1068	      out(`     node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --run --reviewer ${r} \\`);
  1069	      out(`       --packet ${pkt} --subject-sha ${sha}${refs}`);
  1070	    }
  1071	    out(`  ${step++}. Gate it:`);
  1072	    out(`     node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --gate-done --packet ${pkt} \\`);
  1073	    out(`       --subject-sha ${sha}${refs} --run-checks`);
  1074	  }
  1075	  out('');
  1076	  out('Reviewer availability on this machine:');
  1077	  out('  node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --doctor');
  1078	  return EXIT_PASS;
  1079	}
  1080	
  1081	// ── dispatch ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1082	const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
  1083	let code;
  1084	
  1085	// Every command runs inside this guard. A PolicyError means a rule could not be
  1086	// evaluated safely — a missing protected-path policy, an unusable git ref. That
  1087	// is a HARD BLOCK (exit 3), never a warning and never a default, because the
  1088	// alternative is proceeding while blind to what is protected.
  1089	try {
  1090	  if (args.start) code = cmdStart(args);
  1091	  else if (args.subject) code = cmdSubject(args);
  1092	  else if (args.classify) code = cmdClassify(args);
  1093	  else if (args['spec-check']) code = cmdSpecCheck(args);
  1094	  else if (args['validate-review']) code = cmdValidateReview(args);
  1095	  else if (args['gate-done']) code = cmdGateDone(args);
  1096	  else {
  1097	    process.stderr.write(`
  1098	engineering-os.cjs — deterministic half of the AI Engineering OS
  1099	
  1100	  --subject [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--changed <p> ...] [--json]
  1101	        Compute the SUBJECT of this change: the changed paths minus review
  1102	        evidence, and the sha256 every review must bind to.
  1103	
  1104	  --classify [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--changed <p> ...] [--diff-lines N]
  1105	             [--milestone] [--novel] [--json]
  1106	        Which lane, who must review. Classifies the subject. Fails toward FULL.
  1107	
  1108	  --spec-check --packet <packet.json> [--json]
  1109	        Is the intent pinned? Exit 3 if a source is neither pinnable nor
  1110	        explicitly marked "UNVERIFIED:".
  1111	
  1112	  --validate-review <review.json> [...] [--json]
  1113	        Is this record usable evidence?
  1114	
  1115	  --gate-done --subject-sha <sha> [--packet <p>] [--review <r> ...]
  1116	              [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--changed <p> ...]
  1117	              [--run-checks] [--milestone] [--novel] [--json]
  1118	        Fail-closed gate. --subject-sha is MANDATORY. Exit 3 on any unmet rule.
  1119	        Reaches READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION, or READY_FOR_PR when
  1120	        --run-checks actually executed the packet's checks here.
  1121	
  1122	Exit: 0 pass · 2 usage/malformed · 3 hard-block
  1123	`);
  1124	    code = EXIT_USAGE;
  1125	  }
  1126	} catch (e) {
  1127	  if (e instanceof PolicyError) {
  1128	    process.stderr.write(`
  1129	ENGINEERING-OS HARD-BLOCK
  1130	  rule:   ENGOS-POLICY-UNAVAILABLE
  1131	  reason: ${e.message}
  1132	
  1133	Nothing was evaluated. This fails closed on purpose: a rule that cannot be
  1134	read is not a rule that passes.
  1135	`);
  1136	    code = EXIT_BLOCK;
  1137	  } else {
  1138	    throw e;
  1139	  }
  1140	}
  1141	
  1142	if (args.ledger && (args['gate-done'] || args.classify)) {
  1143	  const stamp = new Date().toISOString();
  1144	  appendLedger({
  1145	    entryId: `LED-ENGOS-${stamp.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}`,
  1146	    ts: stamp,
  1147	    agentId: 'claude-code',
  1148	    gate: 'engineering-os',
  1149	    status: code === EXIT_PASS ? 'PASS' : 'BLOCKED',
  1150	    notes: `engineering-os exit ${code}`,
  1151	  });
  1152	}
  1153	
  1154	process.exit(code);
     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * ledger-writer.cjs  — Builder Control System v1, Module 3
     4	 *
     5	 * Usage:
     6	 *   --append <entry.json>          Validate entry against ledger-entry.schema.json,
     7	 *                                  then append it to builder-control/ledger.json.
     8	 *                                  NEVER mutates or truncates existing entries.
     9	 *   --migrate-policy-ledger        Migrate the single policy/ledger.json record into
    10	 *                                  builder-control/ledger.json as a back-dated IMPORTED
    11	 *                                  entry. Leaves policy/ledger.json as a stub.
    12	 *                                  Safe to re-run: idempotent (checks if already migrated).
    13	 *
    14	 * Invariant: the ledger file is a JSON array. Every operation that touches the file
    15	 * reads the current array, validates no mutation occurred, then writes
    16	 * [  ...existingEntries, newEntry  ]. It never truncates.
    17	 *
    18	 * No npm deps — only Node built-ins + shared hand-rolled JSON-Schema validator.
    19	 */
    20	'use strict';
    21	
    22	const fs   = require('fs');
    23	const path = require('path');
    24	const os   = require('os');
    25	
    26	// ─── Paths ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    27	// The canonical ledger. Tests need an ISOLATED ledger — asserting absolute
    28	// counts against the shared production file is what made ledger-atomicity fail
    29	// under a parallel runner, and its restore-on-exit was destroying other suites'
    30	// legitimately written entries.
    31	//
    32	// The override is deliberately CONSTRAINED to the OS temp directory. Allowing an
    33	// arbitrary path would turn this into an evidence-hiding switch: point the
    34	// ledger somewhere else and gate decisions stop being recorded where anyone
    35	// looks. Confining it to a temp dir makes it useful for isolation and useless
    36	// for redirecting production evidence into another repository path.
    37	function resolveLedgerFile() {
    38	  const override = process.env.AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE;
    39	  if (!override) return path.join(__dirname, 'ledger.json');
    40	  const abs = path.resolve(override);
    41	  const tmpRoot = fs.realpathSync(os.tmpdir());
    42	  let real;
    43	  try { real = fs.realpathSync(path.dirname(abs)); } catch { real = path.dirname(abs); }
    44	  if (real !== tmpRoot && !real.startsWith(tmpRoot + path.sep)) {
    45	    throw new Error(
    46	      `AEGIS_LEDGER_FILE must point inside ${tmpRoot} (got ${abs}). ` +
    47	      'Redirecting the canonical ledger outside a temp directory would hide gate decisions.'
    48	    );
    49	  }
    50	  return abs;
    51	}
    52	const LEDGER_FILE   = resolveLedgerFile();
    53	const SCHEMA_FILE   = path.join(__dirname, 'schemas', 'ledger-entry.schema.json');
    54	const POLICY_LEDGER = path.join(__dirname, '..', 'policy', 'ledger.json');
    55	
    56	// ─── Hand-rolled JSON-Schema draft-07 validator (same logic as packet-tools) ─
    57	function validateSchema(schema, data, ctx, defs) {
    58	  ctx  = ctx  || '#';
    59	  defs = defs || schema['$defs'] || schema['definitions'] || {};
    60	
    61	  const errors = [];
    62	
    63	  if (schema['$ref']) {
    64	    const ref = schema['$ref'];
    65	    if (ref.startsWith('#/$defs/')) {
    66	      const defKey = ref.slice('#/$defs/'.length);
    67	      if (!defs[defKey]) { errors.push(`${ctx}: unresolved $ref ${ref}`); return errors; }
    68	      return validateSchema(defs[defKey], data, ctx, defs);
    69	    }
    70	    errors.push(`${ctx}: unsupported $ref ${ref}`);
    71	    return errors;
    72	  }
    73	
    74	  if (schema.type) {
    75	    const types = Array.isArray(schema.type) ? schema.type : [schema.type];
    76	    const actual = data === null ? 'null' : Array.isArray(data) ? 'array' : typeof data;
    77	    // JSON-Schema: 'integer' means a number with no fractional part
    78	    const typesForCheck = types.map(t => t === 'integer' ? 'number' : t);
    79	    if (!typesForCheck.includes(actual)) {
    80	      errors.push(`${ctx}: expected type ${types.join('|')} but got ${actual}`);
    81	      return errors;
    82	    }
    83	    // If type is 'integer', also verify it's a whole number
    84	    if (types.includes('integer') && actual === 'number' && !Number.isInteger(data)) {
    85	      errors.push(`${ctx}: expected integer but got non-integer number ${data}`);
    86	      return errors;
    87	    }
    88	  }
    89	
    90	  if (schema.required && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    91	    for (const req of schema.required) {
    92	      if (!(req in data)) errors.push(`${ctx}: missing required property "${req}"`);
    93	    }
    94	  }
    95	
    96	  if (schema.properties && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    97	    for (const [key, subSchema] of Object.entries(schema.properties)) {
    98	      if (key in data) {
    99	        errors.push(...validateSchema(subSchema, data[key], `${ctx}/${key}`, defs));
   100	      }
   101	    }
   102	  }
   103	
   104	  if (schema.additionalProperties === false && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
   105	    const allowed = new Set(Object.keys(schema.properties || {}));
   106	    for (const key of Object.keys(data)) {
   107	      if (!allowed.has(key)) errors.push(`${ctx}: unexpected additional property "${key}"`);
   108	    }
   109	  }
   110	
   111	  if (schema.pattern && typeof data === 'string') {
   112	    if (!new RegExp(schema.pattern).test(data)) {
   113	      errors.push(`${ctx}: string does not match pattern ${schema.pattern}`);
   114	    }
   115	  }
   116	
   117	  if (schema.enum && !schema.enum.includes(data)) {
   118	    errors.push(`${ctx}: must be one of ${JSON.stringify(schema.enum)}, got ${JSON.stringify(data)}`);
   119	  }
   120	
   121	  if (typeof schema.minItems === 'number' && Array.isArray(data) && data.length < schema.minItems) {
   122	    errors.push(`${ctx}: array has ${data.length} items, minimum is ${schema.minItems}`);
   123	  }
   124	
   125	  if (schema.items && Array.isArray(data)) {
   126	    data.forEach((item, idx) => {
   127	      errors.push(...validateSchema(schema.items, item, `${ctx}[${idx}]`, defs));
   128	    });
   129	  }
   130	
   131	  if ('const' in schema && data !== schema.const) {
   132	    errors.push(`${ctx}: expected const ${JSON.stringify(schema.const)}, got ${JSON.stringify(data)}`);
   133	  }
   134	
   135	  return errors;
   136	}
   137	
   138	// ─── Load JSON file ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   139	function loadJSON(filePath) {
   140	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8'));
   141	}
   142	
   143	// ─── Read the current ledger array (creates empty array if file absent) ────────
   144	function readLedger() {
   145	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE)) return [];
   146	  const raw = fs.readFileSync(LEDGER_FILE, 'utf8').trim();
   147	  if (!raw) return [];
   148	  const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
   149	  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) {
   150	    throw new Error(`Ledger at ${LEDGER_FILE} is not a JSON array — aborting to prevent data loss`);
   151	  }
   152	  return parsed;
   153	}
   154	
   155	// ─── Atomic, idempotent ledger append ────────────────────────────────────────
   156	// CONFIRMED FINDING #7 (CRITICAL): the previous writeLedger() built
   157	// [...oldEntries, newEntry] from an in-memory snapshot, then checked only that
   158	// the on-disk array was not SHORTER than that snapshot. A concurrent append
   159	// made the file longer, the check passed, and the stale array was written over
   160	// the top — silently destroying the other process's entry. In an append-only
   161	// evidence ledger, a lost entry is a lost gate decision.
   162	//
   163	// The repair has three parts, each closing a distinct hole:
   164	//   1. an exclusive lock, so two appends cannot interleave at all;
   165	//   2. re-reading the CURRENT file under that lock, so the base is never stale;
   166	//   3. write-temp-then-rename, so a crash mid-write cannot leave a half-file.
   167	//
   168	// Idempotency is separate: a repeated operationId is a NO-OP rather than a
   169	// second entry, which is what makes an external retry safe instead of
   170	// duplicating the effect it is retrying.
   171	
   172	const LOCK_FILE = LEDGER_FILE + '.lock';
   173	const LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000;
   174	const LOCK_STALE_MS = 60000;
   175	
   176	function acquireLock() {
   177	  const deadline = Date.now() + LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS;
   178	  for (;;) {
   179	    try {
   180	      const fd = fs.openSync(LOCK_FILE, 'wx');
   181	      fs.writeFileSync(fd, String(process.pid));
   182	      return fd;
   183	    } catch (e) {
   184	      if (e.code !== 'EEXIST') throw e;
   185	      // A lock left behind by a killed process must not wedge the system
   186	      // forever, but "stale" has to be generous enough that a slow-but-alive
   187	      // writer is never stolen from.
   188	      try {
   189	        const age = Date.now() - fs.statSync(LOCK_FILE).mtimeMs;
   190	        if (age > LOCK_STALE_MS) { fs.unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE); continue; }
   191	      } catch { /* raced with the holder releasing it */ }
   192	      if (Date.now() > deadline) {
   193	        throw new Error(`could not acquire the ledger lock within ${LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS}ms (held by another process). Refusing to write rather than risk clobbering it.`);
   194	      }
   195	      // Busy-wait briefly. Deliberately synchronous: this whole module is a
   196	      // synchronous CLI, and an async lock here would change its contract.
   197	      const until = Date.now() + 25;
   198	      while (Date.now() < until) { /* spin */ }
   199	    }
   200	  }
   201	}
   202	
   203	function releaseLock(fd) {
   204	  try { fs.closeSync(fd); } catch { /* already closed */ }
   205	  try { fs.unlinkSync(LOCK_FILE); } catch { /* already gone */ }
   206	}
   207	
   208	// Append under an exclusive lock. Returns {appended, length, duplicate}.
   209	function appendAtomic(newEntry) {
   210	  const fd = acquireLock();
   210	  const fd = acquireLock();
   211	  try {
   212	    // Re-read INSIDE the lock. This is the fix: the base is whatever is on
   213	    // disk right now, never a snapshot taken before the lock was held.
   214	    const current = fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE) ? readLedger() : [];
   215	
   216	    if (newEntry.operationId) {
   217	      const existing = current.find((e) => e && e.operationId === newEntry.operationId);
   218	      if (existing) {
   219	        return { appended: false, duplicate: true, length: current.length, existingEntryId: existing.entryId };
   220	      }
   221	    }
   222	    if (newEntry.entryId && current.some((e) => e && e.entryId === newEntry.entryId)) {
   223	      throw new Error(`entryId "${newEntry.entryId}" already exists in the ledger. Entry ids must be unique.`);
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    const next = [...current, newEntry];
   227	    const tmp = LEDGER_FILE + '.tmp-' + process.pid;
   228	    fs.writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(next, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   229	    fs.renameSync(tmp, LEDGER_FILE); // atomic within a filesystem
   230	    return { appended: true, duplicate: false, length: next.length };
   231	  } finally {
   232	    releaseLock(fd);
   233	  }
   234	}
   235	
   236	// Retained for callers that still expect the old name. It now delegates to the
   237	// atomic path and ignores the caller's stale snapshot entirely, because using
   238	// that snapshot as the write base was the defect.
   239	function writeLedger(_oldEntriesIgnored, newEntry) {
   240	  return appendAtomic(newEntry).length;
   241	}
   242	
   243	// ─── Validate an entry object against the ledger-entry schema ─────────────────
   244	function validateEntry(entry) {
   245	  const schema = loadJSON(SCHEMA_FILE);
   246	  const defs   = schema['$defs'] || {};
   247	  return validateSchema(schema, entry, '#', defs);
   248	}
   249	
   250	// ─── --append <entry.json> ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   251	function appendEntry(entryPath) {
   252	  // Load entry
   253	  let entry;
   254	  try {
   255	    entry = loadJSON(path.resolve(entryPath));
   256	  } catch (e) {
   257	    console.error(`ERROR loading entry file: ${e.message}`);
   258	    process.exit(2);
   259	  }
   260	
   261	  // Validate
   262	  const errors = validateEntry(entry);
   263	  if (errors.length) {
   264	    console.error('SCHEMA VALIDATION FAILED for entry:');
   265	    errors.forEach(e => console.error(`  • ${e}`));
   266	    process.exit(1);
   267	  }
   268	
   269	  // Append atomically. A repeated operationId is reported as a no-op rather
   270	  // than written twice — the caller needs to know its retry was absorbed.
   271	  let res;
   272	  try {
   273	    res = appendAtomic(entry);
   274	  } catch (e) {
   275	    console.error(`[ledger-writer] REFUSED: ${e.message}`);
   276	    process.exit(1);
   277	  }
   278	  if (res.duplicate) {
   279	    console.log(`[ledger-writer] NO-OP: operationId "${entry.operationId}" is already recorded as "${res.existingEntryId}".`);
   280	    console.log('[ledger-writer] The retry was absorbed; no duplicate effect was written.');
   281	    process.exit(0);
   282	  }
   283	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Appended entry "${entry.entryId}" to ${LEDGER_FILE}`);
   284	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Ledger length: ${res.length - 1} → ${res.length}  (grew by 1)`);
   285	  process.exit(0);
   286	}
   287	
   288	// ─── --migrate-policy-ledger ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   289	function migratePolicyLedger() {
   290	  // 1. Read the policy ledger
   291	  if (!fs.existsSync(POLICY_LEDGER)) {
   292	    console.error(`ERROR: policy/ledger.json not found at ${POLICY_LEDGER}`);
   293	    process.exit(2);
   294	  }
   295	
   296	  let source;
   297	  try {
   298	    source = loadJSON(POLICY_LEDGER);
   299	  } catch (e) {
   300	    console.error(`ERROR parsing policy/ledger.json: ${e.message}`);
   301	    process.exit(2);
   302	  }
   303	
   304	  // Check if already a stub (migration already done)
   305	  if (source && source._stub === true) {
   306	    console.log('[ledger-writer] policy/ledger.json is already a stub — migration already complete.');
   307	    process.exit(0);
   308	  }
   309	
   310	  // 2. Extract the single record
   311	  // Shape: { "agent:main:main": { artifacts: { research_report: [ { ts, source: { via, targetAgent, targetRole } } ] } } }
   312	  const agentKey   = 'agent:main:main';
   313	  const agentBlock = source[agentKey];
   314	
   315	  if (!agentBlock) {
   316	    console.error(`ERROR: Expected key "${agentKey}" in policy/ledger.json — STOP (stopCondition: migrated record would lose data)`);
   317	    process.exit(2);
   318	  }
   319	
   320	  const artifacts = agentBlock.artifacts || {};
   321	  const reports   = artifacts.research_report || [];
   322	
   323	  if (reports.length === 0) {
   324	    console.error('ERROR: No research_report entries found — STOP (would lose data)');
   325	    process.exit(2);
   326	  }
   327	
   328	  // We migrate ONE record as specified (the contract says "the single existing record")
   329	  const original = reports[0];
   330	  const via          = original.source && original.source.via         ? original.source.via         : null;
   331	  const targetAgent  = original.source && original.source.targetAgent ? original.source.targetAgent : null;
   332	  const targetRole   = original.source && original.source.targetRole  ? original.source.targetRole  : null;
   333	  const origTs       = original.ts || '2026-04-16T14:02:52.750Z';
   334	
   335	  // 3. Check if already migrated in ledger (idempotency)
   336	  const existing = readLedger();
   337	  const alreadyMigrated = existing.some(e => e.gate === 'legacy-import' && e.notes && e.notes.includes('agent:main:main'));
   338	  if (alreadyMigrated) {
   339	    console.log('[ledger-writer] Entry for agent:main:main already present in ledger — skipping duplicate migration.');
   340	    process.exit(0);
   341	  }
   342	
   343	  // 4. Build the IMPORTED ledger entry
   344	  // Preserve ALL original fields in notes so no data is lost.
   345	  const preservedOriginal = JSON.stringify(original);
   346	
   347	  const importedEntry = {
   348	    entryId:       'LED-LEGACY-IMPORT-0001',
   349	    ts:            new Date().toISOString(),               // migration timestamp (now)
   350	    packetId:      null,                                   // no packet existed at original write time
   351	    agentId:       targetAgent || 'daniel',               // the original target agent
   352	    gate:          'legacy-import',
   353	    status:        'IMPORTED',
   354	    blockRule:     null,
   355	    operation:     `research_report dispatch via ${via || 'sessions_spawn'} to ${targetAgent || 'daniel'} (back-dated ${origTs})`,
   356	    changed:       [],
   357	    commandsRun:   [],
   358	    testsRun:      [],
   359	    screenshots:   [],
   360	    commitSha:     null,
   361	    bundleHash:    null,
   362	    evidencePaths: [],
   363	    driftChecks:   [],
   364	    notes: [
   365	      `MIGRATED from policy/ledger.json on ${new Date().toISOString()}.`,
   366	      `Original key: agent:main:main`,
   367	      `Original ts: ${origTs}`,
   368	      `Original via: ${via}`,
   369	      `Original targetAgent: ${targetAgent}`,
   370	      `Original targetRole: ${targetRole}`,
   371	      `Full original record: ${preservedOriginal}`
   372	    ].join(' | ')
   373	  };
   374	
   375	  // 5. Validate the entry before writing
   376	  const errors = validateEntry(importedEntry);
   377	  if (errors.length) {
   378	    console.error('STOP: migrated entry fails schema validation — not writing (data preserved in policy/ledger.json):');
   379	    errors.forEach(e => console.error(`  • ${e}`));
   380	    process.exit(1);
   381	  }
   382	
   383	  // 6. Append to ledger
   384	  const newLen = writeLedger(existing, importedEntry);
   385	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Migrated entry written to ${LEDGER_FILE}`);
   386	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Ledger length: ${existing.length} → ${newLen}`);
   387	
   388	  // 7. Leave policy/ledger.json as a stub pointing to the canonical ledger
   389	  const stub = {
   390	    _stub:      true,
   391	    _canonical: 'builder-control/ledger.json',
   392	    _migrated:  new Date().toISOString(),
   393	    _note:      'This file is a stub. The single record originally here was migrated to builder-control/ledger.json as entryId LED-LEGACY-IMPORT-0001. Do not delete this file or edit builder-control/ledger.json directly — use ledger-writer.cjs --append.'
   394	  };
   395	  fs.writeFileSync(POLICY_LEDGER, JSON.stringify(stub, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   396	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] policy/ledger.json → stub written (canonical: builder-control/ledger.json)`);
   397	
   398	  // 8. Print the migrated entry for anti-theater verification
   399	  console.log('\n[ledger-writer] Migrated entry:');
   400	  console.log(JSON.stringify(importedEntry, null, 2));
   401	
   402	  process.exit(0);
   403	}
   404	
   405	// ─── CLI dispatch ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   406	// Guarded so this file can be require()d as a module. It previously ran the CLI
   407	// (and process.exit'd) on import, which meant any code trying to reuse
   408	// appendAtomic() killed itself printing a usage message.
   409	const args = process.argv.slice(2);
   410	const cmd  = args[0];
   411	
   412	if (require.main !== module) {
   413	  // imported as a library — export only, run nothing
   414	} else if (cmd === '--append') {
   415	  const entryPath = args[1];
   416	  if (!entryPath) {
   417	    console.error('Usage: node ledger-writer.cjs --append <entry.json>');
   418	    process.exit(2);
   419	  }
   420	  appendEntry(entryPath);
   421	
   422	} else if (cmd === '--migrate-policy-ledger') {
   423	  migratePolicyLedger();
   424	
   425	} else {
   426	  console.error('Usage:');
   427	  console.error('  node ledger-writer.cjs --append <entry.json>');
   428	  console.error('  node ledger-writer.cjs --migrate-policy-ledger');
   429	  process.exit(2);
   430	}
   431	
   432	module.exports = { appendAtomic, acquireLock, releaseLock, readLedger, validateEntry };

   191	
   192	// ── engineering state (the gate's own verdict — never re-judged here) ───────
   193	function projectEngineering(args) {
   194	  const a = [ENGOS, '--gate-done', '--json'];
   195	  if (args.base) a.push('--base', args.base);
   196	  if (args.head) a.push('--head', args.head);
   197	  if (args.diffLines) a.push('--diff-lines', String(args.diffLines));
   198	  for (const c of args.changed || []) a.push('--changed', c);
   199	  if (args.packet) a.push('--packet', args.packet);
   200	  if (args.subjectSha) a.push('--subject-sha', args.subjectSha);
   201	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   202	
   203	  // A BLOCKED gate exits 3. That is a successful reading of a blocked system,
   204	  // not a failure to read — so it is projected, not swallowed.
   205	  let parsed = null;
   206	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* handled below */ }
   207	  if (!parsed) {
   208	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   209	      reason: `could not read a gate verdict (exit ${r.status}): ${(r.stderr || '').trim().split('\n')[0] || 'no output'}`,
   210	      source: rel(ENGOS) };
   211	  }
   212	
   213	  const stageEvidence = deriveStages(parsed);
   214	  return {
   215	    state: 'OK',
   216	    gateExit: r.status,
   217	    verdict: parsed.ok ? parsed.state : 'BLOCKED',
   218	    lane: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : 'UNKNOWN',
   219	    highRisk: parsed.classification ? !!parsed.classification.highRisk : null,
   220	    requiredReviewers: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.requiredReviewers : [],
   221	    subjectSha256: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectSha256 : null,
   222	    subjectPaths: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectPaths : [],
   223	    excludedAsEvidence: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.excludedAsEvidence : [],
   224	    problems: parsed.problems || [],
   225	    observed: parsed.observed || [],
   226	    unverified: parsed.unverified || [],
   227	    stages: stageEvidence,
   228	    source: rel(ENGOS),
   229	  };
   230	}
   231	
   232	// Map the gate verdict onto the declared stage sequence. This does not decide
   233	// anything — it re-expresses the gate's own problems as stage states so the
   234	// drift is drawable. A stage with no positive evidence is UNVERIFIED, which is
   235	// distinct from FAILED: nobody looked, versus somebody looked and it failed.
   236	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   237	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   238	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   239	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   240	
   241	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   242	    switch (id) {
   243	      case 'spec':
   244	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   245	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   246	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   247	      case 'build':
   248	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (codex REV-…-codex, CRITICAL/HIGH set, #8): this
   249	        // returned PASS unconditionally. A diff existing is not evidence that
   250	        // it builds — nothing in a gate verdict speaks to compilation at all.
   251	        // Rendering PASS here was the projector fabricating a positive state,
   252	        // which is precisely what the amendment forbids.
   253	        if (!parsed.subject || !(parsed.subject.subjectPaths || []).length) {
   254	          return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'no subject paths — there is nothing here to build' };
   255	        }
   256	        if (/deterministic checks executed here: (\d+)\/\1 passed/.test(observed)) {
   257	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate executed the declared checks and they passed' };
   258	        }
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'a subject diff exists, but nothing in this verdict verifies it builds' };
   260	      case 'deterministic':
   261	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   262	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   263	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   264	      case 'codex':
   265	      case 'grok': {
   266	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   267	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   268	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   269	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   270	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   271	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (#8): this used to fall through to PASS after
   272	        // checking only three failure shapes. Any rule the checks above did not
   273	        // recognise — a stale binding, short coverage, ambiguous records, a
   274	        // wrong packet — silently rendered as an approval. Absence of a known
   275	        // failure is not evidence of a pass. Require the gate to have positively
   276	        // observed this reviewer's disposition instead.
   277	        if (new RegExp(`(^|\\|)\\s*${id}: (APPROVE|APPROVE_WITH_NOTES)`, 'm').test(observed)) {
   278	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate observed an approval bound to this subject' };
   279	        }
   280	        if (problems.some((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail))) {
   281	          const hit = problems.find((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail));
   282	          return { state: 'FAILED', reason: hit.rule + ': ' + String(hit.detail).slice(0, 120) };
   283	        }
   284	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no approval was observed for this reviewer on this subject' };
   285	      }
   286	      case 'copilot':
   287	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   288	      case 'gate':
   289	        return parsed.ok
   290	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   291	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   292	      case 'runtime':
   293	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   294	      case 'checkpoint':
   295	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   296	      default:
   297	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   298	    }
   299	  };
   300	
   301	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   302	}
   303	
   304	// ── recent events (the ledger IS the event bus — no second store) ───────────
   305	function projectEvents(limit) {
   306	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER)) {
   307	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger not found at ${rel(LEDGER)}`, events: [] };
   308	  }
   309	  let l;
   310	  try { l = readJSON(LEDGER); }
   311	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger unreadable: ${e.message}`, events: [] }; }
   312	  if (!Array.isArray(l)) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'ledger is not an array', events: [] };
   313	  const events = l.slice(-limit).reverse().map((e) => ({
   314	    entryId: e.entryId, ts: e.ts, gate: e.gate, status: e.status,
   315	    agentId: e.agentId, packetId: e.packetId || null,
   316	    blockRule: e.blockRule || null,
   317	    operation: e.operation || null,
   318	  }));
   319	  return { state: 'OK', total: l.length, events, source: rel(LEDGER) };
   320	}
   321	
   322	// ── snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   323	function snapshot(args) {
   324	  const nowIso = new Date().toISOString();
   325	  const now = Date.parse(nowIso);
   326	  const engineering = projectEngineering(args);
   327	  const integration = { connectors: projectConnectors(now) };
   328	
   329	  // Failure isolation, made structural: these are two objects. There is no
   330	  // combined "overall health" field, because the moment one exists somebody
   331	  // will let a failed connector darken a verified build.
   332	  return {
   333	    generatedAt: nowIso,
   334	    contract: {
   335	      absences: ['UNAVAILABLE', 'STALE', 'UNVERIFIED'],
   336	      note: 'Engineering and integration state are separate and are never blended. No field in this snapshot is synthesised; every value cites the artifact it came from.',
   337	    },
   338	    engineering,
   339	    integration,
   340	    // A THIRD plane, deliberately not merged into either of the others. It is
   341	    // not engineering (it holds no authority) and not integration (a Notion
   342	    // disagreement is not a connector fault). Separating it is precisely what
   343	    // makes "engineering VERIFIED / knowledge CONFLICT" a renderable pair
   344	    // rather than a contradiction something has to resolve.
   345	    knowledge: (() => {
   346	      try { return require('./knowledge-mirror.cjs').project({ now }); }
   347	      catch (e) {
   348	        // A refusal is a real, reportable state — never silently omitted.
   349	        return { state: 'REFUSED', reason: e.message, records: [], conflicts: 0 };
   350	      }
   351	    })(),
   352	    reviewers: projectReviewers(now),
   353	    events: projectEvents(Number(args.events) > 0 ? Number(args.events) : 12),
   354	  };
   355	}
   356	
   357	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   358	function parseArgs(argv) {
   359	  const a = {};
   360	  // CONFIRMED FINDING #8: the projector only accepted --packet and
   361	  // --subject-sha, so gateDone recomputed from `git diff HEAD` — empty once the
   362	  // reviewed change is committed. The dashboard then showed an empty subject
   363	  // for work that had actually been reviewed. The exact range the gate used is
   364	  // now forwarded rather than reconstructed from the working tree.
   365	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   366	    const t = argv[i];
   367	    if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   368	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   369	    else if (t === '--events') a.events = argv[++i];
   370	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   371	    else if (t === '--out') a.out = argv[++i];
   372	  }
   373	  return a;
   374	}
   375	
   376	if (require.main === module) {
   377	  const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
   378	  let snap;
   379	  try {
   380	    snap = snapshot(args);
   381	  } catch (e) {
   382	    if (e.refused) {
   383	      process.stderr.write(`\nAEGIS-STATE REFUSED\n  rule:   AEGIS-CONNECTOR-PLANE-VIOLATION\n  reason: ${e.message}\n`);
   384	      process.exit(EXIT_REFUSED);
   385	    }
   386	    throw e;
   387	  }
   388	  const text = JSON.stringify(snap, null, 2);
   389	  if (args.out) {
   390	    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(args.out), { recursive: true });
   391	    // A `.js` target emits a global assignment rather than bare JSON. The
   392	    // dashboard has to work from file:// with no server — that is the whole
   393	    // point of an evidence view — and fetch() of a local JSON file is blocked
   394	    // by the file:// origin policy. A script tag is not. The content is
   395	    // identical either way; only the wrapper differs.
   396	    const isJs = args.out.endsWith('.js');
   397	    const body = isJs
   398	      ? `/* Generated by builder-control/aegis-state.cjs — do not edit by hand.\n   Every value here cites the artifact it came from. Regenerate with:\n     node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js */\nwindow.AEGIS_STATE = ${text};\n`
   399	      : text + '\n';
   400	    fs.writeFileSync(args.out, body, 'utf8');
   401	    process.stderr.write(`[aegis-state] wrote ${rel(path.resolve(args.out))}\n`);
   402	  } else {
   403	    process.stdout.write(text + '\n');
   404	  }
   405	  process.exit(EXIT_PASS);
   340	// ignored the Tool Capability Canon, so disabling a reviewer in the canon had
   341	// no effect on what actually ran, and dashboard/router state could disagree
   342	// with execution. The canon is now consulted before any tool is launched.
   343	// CONFIRMED FINDING #5: canonGate only rejected enabled=false / DISABLED, so a
   344	// tool whose availability was UNVERIFIED or BLOCKED still ran, and runTool()
   345	// spawned Grok directly without ever consulting routeRole() — meaning the
   346	// metered authorization, data-class veto and role separation in
   347	// MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json governed nothing at execution time. There is now
   348	// ONE authorization path and every launch goes through it.
   349	function authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts = {}) {
   350	  const canon = canonGate(reviewer);
   351	  if (!canon.ok) return canon;
   352	
   353	  // Availability must be positively AVAILABLE with evidence. UNVERIFIED is not
   354	  // a soft yes — it is the absence of a check.
   355	  const avail = canon.tool.availability;
   356	  if (avail !== 'AVAILABLE') {
   357	    return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} availability is ${avail} in the Tool Capability Canon; only AVAILABLE (with evidence) may be launched` };
   358	  }
   359	  if (!canon.tool.availabilityEvidence || !canon.tool.availabilityEvidence.observedAt) {
   360	    return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is marked AVAILABLE but carries no dated availability evidence` };
   361	  }
   362	
   363	  // Role routing: data-class veto, metered authorization, role separation.
   364	  const roleId = reviewer === 'grok' ? 'adversarial-review'
   365	    : reviewer === 'codex' ? 'implementation-review'
   366	    : 'repository-guardian';
   367	  let route;
   368	  try {
   369	    route = require('./tool-router.cjs').routeRole(roleId, {
   370	      dataClass: opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL',
   371	      allowMetered: opts.allowMetered,
   372	      approvedBy: opts.approvedBy,
   373	      capUsd: opts.capUsd,
   374	    });
   375	  } catch (e) {
   376	    return { ok: false, reason: `model routing policy unusable: ${e.message}` };
   377	  }
   378	  if (!route.ok) return { ok: false, reason: `${route.code}: ${route.reason}` };
   379	  return { ok: true, tool: canon.tool, route };
   380	}
   381	
   382	function canonGate(reviewer) {
   383	  const fsx = require('fs');
   384	  const canonPath = require('path').join(HERE, 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json');
   385	  if (!fsx.existsSync(canonPath)) {
   386	    return { ok: false, reason: 'TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json is missing — refusing to run a reviewer the canon cannot authorize' };
   387	  }
   388	  let canon;
   389	  try { canon = JSON.parse(fsx.readFileSync(canonPath, 'utf8')); }
   390	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, reason: `tool canon unreadable: ${e.message}` }; }
   391	  const wanted = { codex: 'codex-local', grok: 'grok-cli' }[reviewer];
   392	  const tool = (canon.tools || []).find((x) => x.toolId === wanted);
   393	  if (!tool) return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is not declared in the Tool Capability Canon` };
   394	  if (tool.enabled === false) return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} is DISABLED in the Tool Capability Canon` };
   395	  if (tool.availability === 'DISABLED') return { ok: false, reason: `${reviewer} availability is DISABLED in the canon` };
   396	  return { ok: true, tool };
   397	}
   398	
   399	// Build the exact argument vector a reviewer will be launched with. Exported
   400	// so a test can assert that every bound the policy DECLARES is actually
   401	// PRESENT on the command line — the gap that let maxTurns:1 run 13 turns.
   402	function buildToolArgv(reviewer, prompt, bounds) {
   403	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   404	    return ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   405	  }
   406	  const argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   407	  if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   408	  if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   409	  return argv;
   410	}
   411	
   412	function runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec, opts = {}) {
   413	  const t = TOOLS[reviewer];
   414	  if (!t) return { ok: false, reason: `unknown reviewer ${reviewer}` };
   415	  const gateResult = authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts);
   416	  if (!gateResult.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gateResult.reason, raw: '' };
   417	  if (!isExecutable(t.bin)) {
   418	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} is not executable at ${t.bin}`, raw: '' };
   419	  }
   420	  let argv;
   421	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   422	    // read-only sandbox; the model cannot write regardless of what it decides.
   423	    argv = ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   424	  } else {
   425	    // Bounds come from MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json, not from this file, so the
   426	    // Product Owner can tighten an adversarial reviewer without editing code.
   427	    // Single-turn print mode, no approval flags, no web, no sub-agents: the
   428	    // adversary gets exactly one pass at the diff and no way to spend the
   429	    // budget on a conversation with itself.
   430	    argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   431	    let bounds = null;
   432	    try {
   433	      bounds = (require('./tool-router.cjs').loadPolicy().roles['adversarial-review'] || {}).bounds || null;
   434	    } catch { /* absent policy is handled by the router's own refusal */ }
   435	    // CONFIRMED DEFECT (2026-08-24, caught by reading a real run's telemetry):
   436	    // the policy declared maxTurns:1 and this adapter never passed it. Grok ran
   437	    // 13 turns and returned no record. A bound that is declared in a policy but
   438	    // not passed to the tool is theatre — it reads as governance in the JSON and
   439	    // enforces nothing at runtime. Every bound below is now wired to a real flag
   440	    // or refuses, and buildToolArgv() is asserted against the policy by test.
   441	    if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) {
   442	      argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   443	    }
   444	    if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   445	    // Sub-agents are disabled by OMITTING --agents entirely. Passing an empty
   446	    // value was a bug: the CLI expects a map and rejected the array outright,
   447	    // which failed the launch at argument parsing. No definitions supplied is
   448	    // the correct way to express "no sub-agents".
   449	    if (bounds && bounds.subagents === true) {
   450	      throw new Error('policy enables sub-agents for adversarial-review; this adapter deliberately does not implement that path');
   451	    }
   452	  }
   453	  const r = spawnSync(t.bin, argv, {
   454	    encoding: 'utf8',
   455	    timeout: timeoutSec * 1000,
   456	    maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024,
   457	    cwd: ROOT,
   458	  });
   459	  const raw = (r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr ? `\n--- stderr ---\n${r.stderr}` : '');
   460	  if (r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') {
   461	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exceeded the ${timeoutSec}s timeout`, raw };
   462	  }
   463	  if (r.error) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} failed to start: ${r.error.message}`, raw };
   464	  if (r.status !== 0) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exited ${r.status}`, raw };
   465	  return { ok: true, raw };
   466	}
   467

   470	  if (!reviewer) return usage('--reviewer is required');
