   152	            # gate entirely, so the fallback silently disabled enforcement on
   153	            # exactly the runs that needed it. HEAD's own file list is now
   154	            # enumerated explicitly, and an empty result fails closed below.
   155	            echo "Base '$BASE' unavailable — enumerating HEAD's own files."
   156	            USED_BASE=""
   157	            HEADFILES=$(git diff-tree --root --no-commit-id -r --name-only HEAD | sed '/^$/d')
   158	            if [ -z "$HEADFILES" ]; then
   159	              echo "BLOCKED: no trustworthy file range could be established for HEAD."
   160	              echo "An unknown subject is never treated as a small one."
   161	              exit 1
   162	            fi
   163	            CHANGEDARGS=()
   164	            while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && CHANGEDARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done <<< "$HEADFILES"
   165	            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject "${CHANGEDARGS[@]}" --json)
   166	          else
   167	            USED_BASE="$BASE"
   168	            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject --base "$BASE" --head HEAD --json)
   169	          fi
   170	          echo "$SUBJ"
   171	          SHA=$(echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).subjectSha256))')
   172	          echo "sha=$SHA" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   173	          echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>{const j=JSON.parse(s);console.log(j.subjectPaths.join("\n"))})' > /tmp/subject-paths.txt
   174	          echo "count=$(wc -l < /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr -d " ")" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   175	          # Record the EXACT base used, so the gate step certifies the same
   176	          # range rather than recomputing a different one.
   177	          echo "base=$USED_BASE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   178	
   179	      - name: Engineering OS — bind to exactly one task packet
   180	        # A change that carries two modified packets has two competing
   181	        # authorizations and no deterministic way to choose between them.
   182	        # Rather than pick one, this blocks and says so.
   183	        id: packet
   184	        run: |
   185	          set -euo pipefail
   186	          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
   187	          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
   188	             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
   189	            CHANGED=$(git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r HEAD)
   190	          else
   191	            CHANGED=$(git diff --name-only "$BASE" HEAD)
   192	          fi
   193	          PACKETS=$(echo "$CHANGED" | grep -E '^builder-control/packets/.*\.json$' || true)
   194	          COUNT=$(if [ -z "$PACKETS" ]; then echo 0; else echo "$PACKETS" | wc -l | tr -d ' '; fi)
   195	          echo "changed packets: $COUNT"
   196	          echo "$PACKETS"
   197	          if [ "$COUNT" -gt 1 ]; then
   198	            echo ''
   199	            echo 'BLOCKED: this change modifies more than one task packet.'
   200	            echo 'Exactly one packet authorizes a change. Two packets means two'
   201	            echo 'answers to "what was this allowed to touch", and there is no'
   202	            echo 'honest way to pick. Split the change.'
   203	            exit 1
   204	          fi
   205	          if [ "$COUNT" -eq 1 ]; then
   206	            echo "active=$PACKETS" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   207	            echo "── pinning intent for $PACKETS"
   208	            node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --spec-check --packet "$PACKETS"
   209	          else
   210	            echo "active=" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   211	            echo "No task packet added or modified by this change."
   212	          fi
   213	
   214	      - name: Engineering OS — classify the subject
   215	        id: classify
   216	        run: |
   217	          set -euo pipefail
   218	          SUBJECT_BASE="${{ steps.subject.outputs.base }}"
   219	          ARGS=()
   220	          while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && ARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done < /tmp/subject-paths.txt
   221	          if [ ${#ARGS[@]} -eq 0 ]; then
   222	            # FINDING #2: an empty subject used to be declared LIGHT, which is
   223	            # how a computation failure became a governance bypass. Zero subject
   224	            # paths after a successful subject computation means the change is
   225	            # evidence-only; that is reported, and the lane is left UNKNOWN so
   226	            # the gate step still runs and decides.
   227	            echo "No subject paths — evidence-only change. Lane left UNKNOWN; the gate still runs."
   228	            echo "lane=UNKNOWN" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   229	            exit 0
   230	          fi
   231	          # The classifier now refuses LIGHT without a line count (finding #10),
   232	          # so CI must supply the real one rather than let an unknown size pass.
   233	          if [ -n "$SUBJECT_BASE" ]; then
   234	            LINES=$(git diff --numstat "$SUBJECT_BASE" HEAD -- $(cat /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr '\n' ' ') | awk '{a+=$1+$2} END {print a+0}')
   235	          else
   236	            LINES=$(git diff --numstat HEAD -- $(cat /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr '\n' ' ') | awk '{a+=$1+$2} END {print a+0}')
   237	          fi
   238	          echo "subject changed lines: $LINES"
   239	          node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --classify "${ARGS[@]}" --diff-lines "$LINES" | tee /tmp/classify.txt
   240	          LANE=$(awk -F': *' '/^lane /{print $2; exit}' /tmp/classify.txt | awk '{print $1}')
   241	          echo "lane=$LANE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   242	          {
   243	            echo "### Engineering OS — change classification"
   244	            echo ''
   245	            echo '```'
   246	            cat /tmp/classify.txt
   247	            echo '```'
   248	          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"
   249	
   250	      - name: Engineering OS — required review evidence (binding)
   251	        # Fail-closed. Missing review evidence blocks the merge. A review bound
   252	        # to a different subject hash does not count. An unavailable reviewer
   253	        # blocks and is reported as UNAVAILABLE rather than upgraded to a pass.
   254	        #
   255	        # --subject-sha is passed explicitly and is mandatory in the CLI: a gate
   256	        # decision with no bound subject certifies nothing at all.
   257	        # Runs for FULL and for UNKNOWN. Only a positively-classified LIGHT skips.
   258	        if: steps.classify.outputs.lane != 'LIGHT'
   259	        run: |
   260	          set -euo pipefail
    18	- whether metered spending occurred;
    19	- the last known-good checkpoint and available rollback action.
    20	
    21	The dashboard observes and controls the canonical Workflow Engine. **It does
    22	not own or duplicate workflow state.**
    23	
    24	## 2. Local-first boundary
    25	
    26	The following live on Marc's machine in V1:
    27	
    28	- dashboard application and local control service;
    29	- durable workflow, policy, event, approval, and evidence records;
    30	- SQLite database in WAL mode;
    31	- repositories, isolated worktrees, specifications, and local artifacts;
    32	- watchdog, heartbeats, leases, stale-run detection, and recovery metadata;
    33	- tool/model configuration and budget policy;
    34	- credential references. Secret values belong in macOS Keychain, never the DB,
    35	  prompts, event log, screenshots, or repository.
    36	
    37	GitHub, Notion, and cloud AI/media tools remain external services. Local-first
    38	does not mean local inference: relevant context may leave the machine when an
    39	external provider is used. The router must enforce data-classification limits
    40	and record the provider selected.
    41	
    42	## 3. Durable control plane
    43	
    44	The orchestrator does not remember the state. A durable state machine owns it.
    45	Every transition records actor, timestamp, subject, evidence, and policy
    46	decision. Agents request transitions; policy permits or refuses them.
    47	
    48	Minimum states:
    49	
    50	`PENDING -> ANALYZING -> PLANNED -> IMPLEMENTING -> DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION ->
    51	INDEPENDENT_REVIEW -> CORRECTION -> RUNTIME_VALIDATION -> CHECKPOINTING ->
    52	VERIFIED`
    53	
    54	`BLOCKED`, `PAUSED`, `CANCELLED`, and `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` are explicit states.
    55	Retries are idempotent. A lease prevents two workers owning one step. A
    56	heartbeat and timeout expose abandoned work. Resume starts from durable state,
    57	not conversation memory.
    58	
    59	## 4. Execution modes and money
    60	
    61	Every executable capability declares one mode:
    62	
    63	1. `SUBSCRIPTION_CLI` — preferred when a supported authenticated CLI uses an
    64	   existing subscription allowance.
    65	2. `BUILT_IN` or `PLUGIN_MCP` — preferred when already available in the active
    66	   surface and suitable for the task.
    67	3. `DESKTOP_ASSISTED` — for attended or semi-attended work such as documents,
    68	   Notion, or a provider UI. It is not trusted as the only unattended control
    69	   path.
    70	4. `API_METERED` — reliable headless execution when authorized.
    71	5. `UNAVAILABLE` — honest terminal state when nothing appropriate can run.
    72	
    73	Default metered budget is **$0**. A non-zero metered run requires a named human
    74	approval and a hard cap. Included-plan usage is still tracked because plan
    75	limits are finite.
    76	
    77	## 5. Capability-first tool routing
    78	
    79	AEGIS routes the task to a verified capability before choosing a model.
    80	
    81	- Claude is not a video generator, advertising platform, design application,
    82	  deployment host, or spreadsheet engine merely because it can describe one.
    83	- A specialist-required task cannot fall back silently to a general model.
    84	- Video generation prefers OpenArt when its connector is verified and enabled.
    85	- Advertising requires an enabled advertising specialist selected in the Tool
    86	  Capability Canon. Until one is connected, advertising execution blocks.
    87	- Attachments, MCPs, plugins, APIs, CLIs, desktop tools, and future add-ons are
    88	  all connector types behind the same selection contract.
    89	- Tools may be enabled, disabled, reprioritized, or replaced without rewriting
    90	  workflow doctrine.
    91	
    92	Selection considers task match, availability evidence, data classification,
    93	cost authorization, required inputs, output type, and owner preference. If no
    94	eligible specialist exists, return `SPECIALIST_REQUIRED`, not an improvised
    95	output.
    96	
    97	## 6. Lean workflow
    98	
    99	The ordering rule is **cheapest reliable falsifier first**:
   100	
   101	`objective -> pinned acceptance criteria -> risk -> isolated worktree -> build
   102	-> syntax/type/lint/unit/build checks -> independent review -> bounded
   103	correction -> checks again -> PR/CI -> repository review -> runtime evidence ->
   104	risk-gated red team -> watchdog -> checkpoint -> verified documentation sync`
   105	
   106	Grok is required for high-risk, novel, security-sensitive, control-system, and
   107	milestone work. It is not called for harmless formatting or tiny documentation
   108	changes. Maximum normal correction cycles: three.
   109	
   110	## 7. Tool Capability Canon
   111	
   112	`builder-control/TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json` is the versioned seed catalog for
   113	task-to-tool policy. The live dashboard will store current probe results in its
   114	local DB, but may not silently rewrite the canon.
   115	
   116	Each tool profile names:
   117	
   118	- supported task types and capabilities;
   119	- preferred execution modes;
   120	- availability and evidence;
   121	- strengths and explicit exclusions;
   122	- required inputs and output artifacts;
   123	- cost class, approval rule, and data-classification ceiling;
   124	- verification required before its output is accepted.
   125	
   126	## 8. V1 vertical slice
   127	
   128	V1 proves one complete local loop:
   129	
   130	1. text objective entered;
   131	2. acceptance criteria and risk recorded;
   132	3. tool/model route selected from verified capabilities;
   133	4. isolated worktree created;
   134	5. builder runs;
   135	6. deterministic checks run;
   136	7. independent review binds to the exact subject;
   137	8. corrections are bounded and rechecked;
   138	9. watchdog proves the policy sequence;
   139	10. checkpoint and rollback point recorded;
   140	11. dashboard displays evidence, costs, blockers, and controls.
   141	
   142	Notion begins as approved-intake plus one-way post-checkpoint synchronization.
   143	Bi-directional conflict resolution, voice, cinematic visualization, historical
   144	model learning, and dynamic optimization are later versions.
   145	
   146	## 9. Anti-theatre acceptance
   147	
   148	AEGIS V1 is not complete until the vertical slice is executed end-to-end and a
   149	deliberate negative case proves each control blocks: missing specialist, stale
   150	review, unavailable builder, failed deterministic check, skipped workflow step,
   155	
   156	# AMENDMENT — SCOPE REDUCTION (2026-08-24)
   157	
   158	**Raised by:** Codex finding #11, correction cycle 2 · **Status:** the claimed V1
   159	contract is hereby **reduced** to what is actually built.
   160	
   161	## Why this section exists
   162	
   163	The sections above described a durable control plane: a workflow state machine,
   164	a SQLite WAL store, leases, heartbeats, stale-run recovery, objective intake,
   165	isolated-worktree execution, watchdog sequencing, cost tracking, and
   166	checkpoint/rollback controls. Codex verified against the repository and found
   167	those facilities absent. That is correct.
   168	
   169	There were two honest ways forward — build it, or stop claiming it. Leaving the
   170	contract as written while shipping less is the third option, and it is the one
   171	that produces a document nobody can trust. A contract that overstates what
   172	exists is worse than no contract: it makes every future audit start from a false
   173	baseline.
   174	
   175	So the claim is reduced here, in writing, rather than quietly.
   176	
   177	## What V1 actually is
   178	
   179	**A deterministic evidence gate, plus a read-only projection of it.**
   180	
   181	| built and tested | status |
   182	|---|---|
   183	| subject computation and binding | built |
   184	| lane classification and risk taxonomy | built |
   185	| task packet validation, spec pinning, `filesAllowed` authorization | built |
   186	| review record schema, semantics, and **attestation** | built |
   187	| fail-closed gate with no override | built |
   188	| atomic, idempotent, append-only ledger | built |
   189	| capability/cost/data-class model routing with hard budgets | built |
   190	| read-only reviewer adapters (Codex, Grok) | built |
   191	| knowledge mirror with conflict semantics — **no network** | built |
   192	| connector registry and health projection — **declared, not probed** | built |
   193	| authenticated, data-minimized local dashboard host | built |
   194	
   195	## What V1 is NOT — explicitly withdrawn from the claim
   196	
   197	- **No durable workflow state machine.** State lives in git, the ledger, and
   198	  review records. There is no run table, no lease, no heartbeat.
   199	- **No SQLite WAL store.** The ledger is a JSON file made atomic with a lock.
   200	  Adequate for one machine and one operator; it is not a durable multi-writer
   201	  store and must not be described as one.
   202	- **No stale-run recovery**, because there are no runs to recover.
   203	- **No objective intake.** Work starts from a hand-written task packet.
   204	- **No isolated-worktree execution.** The operator creates the worktree.
   205	- **No watchdog process.** Sequencing is enforced by the gate at decision time,
   206	  not observed by anything at runtime.
   207	- **No cost tracking.** Budgets are enforced *before* a metered call; actual
   208	  spend is not recorded or accumulated. The $0.16 and $0.87 figures in the
   209	  reports were read out of tool output by a human, not tracked by AEGIS.
   210	- **No checkpoint or rollback control.** The rollback point is a git commit an
   211	  operator notes down.
   212	- **No runtime validation.** Nothing here executes the built software and
   213	  observes it behaving correctly. `READY_FOR_PR` is deliberately named to avoid
   214	  implying otherwise.
   215	
   216	## Consequence for the terminal states
   217	
   218	`READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION` and `READY_FOR_PR` mean exactly what they
   219	say: the required process ran and left evidence. Neither means the software
   220	works. With no runtime validation in V1, **no state produced by this system is a
   221	statement about the behaviour of the built product.**
   222	
   223	## Restoring the withdrawn scope
   224	
   225	Each withdrawn item is a real requirement, not a bad idea. Restoring one means
   226	building it and re-approving this contract — not editing this section. Order of
   227	value if resumed: durable run records first (they unblock recovery, cost
   228	tracking, and honest dashboard history at once), then runtime validation, then
   229	checkpoint/rollback, then the watchdog.

   173	// Compute subject paths + the hash reviewers bind to.
   174	// changedPaths may be supplied explicitly; otherwise it is derived from git.
   175	function computeSubject({ base, head, changed }) {
   176	  let changedPaths;
   177	  if (Array.isArray(changed) && changed.length) {
   178	    changedPaths = changed.slice();
   179	  } else {
   180	    const h = assertRef(head || 'HEAD', '--head');
   181	    if (base) {
   182	      const b = assertRef(base, '--base');
   183	      changedPaths = git(['diff', '--name-only', `${b}..${h}`]).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   184	    } else {
   185	      changedPaths = git(['diff', '--name-only', h]).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   186	    }
   187	  }
   188	  changedPaths = [...new Set(changedPaths)].sort();
   189	
   190	  const subjectPaths = changedPaths.filter((p) => !isEvidencePath(p));
   191	  const excluded = changedPaths.filter(isEvidencePath);
   192	
   193	  // Hash ONLY the subject paths. `--` separates paths from refs so a path that
   194	  // looks like a ref cannot be reinterpreted as one.
   195	  let diff = '';
   196	  if (subjectPaths.length) {
   197	    const h = assertRef(head || 'HEAD', '--head');
   198	    const args = ['diff'];
   199	    if (base) args.push(`${assertRef(base, '--base')}..${h}`);
   200	    else args.push(h);
   201	    args.push('--', ...subjectPaths);
   202	    diff = git(args);
   203	  }
   204	
   205	  return {
   206	    changedPaths,
   207	    subjectPaths,
   208	    excludedAsEvidence: excluded,
   209	    subjectSha256: sha256(diff),
   210	    diffBytes: diff.length,
   211	    range: base ? `${base}..${head || 'HEAD'}` : (head || 'HEAD'),
   212	  };
   213	}
   630	  let rec;
   631	  try { rec = readJSON(p); } catch (e) { return { path: p, ok: false, errors: [`not valid JSON: ${e.message}`] }; }
   632	  const schema = readJSON(REVIEW_SCHEMA);
   633	  const errors = [...validateAgainst(schema, rec), ...reviewSemantics(rec)];
   634	
   635	  // CONFIRMED FINDING #1 (CRITICAL): the gate used to trust any JSON found in
   636	  // reviews/. A builder could write {"reviewer":"codex","disposition":"APPROVE"}
   637	  // and satisfy its own independent-review requirement. Attestation is now
   638	  // verified here, so an unsigned or edited record is not evidence at all.
   639	  //
   640	  // Scope, stated honestly: this is a machine-local HMAC. It detects hand-edited
   641	  // verdicts, records copied between subjects, and fabricated approvals dropped
   642	  // into the directory. It does NOT prove a human approved anything and does not
   643	  // defend against a process that can read the key.
   644	  if (!opts.skipAttestation) {
   645	    try {
   646	      const v = require('./review-sign.cjs').verify(rec, { packetPath: opts.packetPath });
   647	      if (!v.ok) errors.push(`${v.code}: ${v.reason}`);
   648	    } catch (e) {
   649	      errors.push(`ATTESTATION-UNVERIFIABLE: ${e.message}`);
   650	    }
   651	  }
   652	  return { path: p, ok: errors.length === 0, errors, rec };
   653	}
   654	
   655	function cmdValidateReview(args) {
   656	  const files = args.review.length ? args.review : args._;
   657	  if (!files.length) die('usage: --validate-review <review.json> [more.json ...]', EXIT_USAGE);
   658	  let bad = 0;
   659	  const results = files.map(loadReview);
   660	  if (args.json) {
   661	    out(JSON.stringify(results.map((r) => ({ path: r.path, ok: r.ok, errors: r.errors })), null, 2));
   662	    return results.every((r) => r.ok) ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_USAGE;
   663	  }
   664	  out('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEW RECORD VALIDATION');
   665	  out('='.repeat(52));
   666	  for (const r of results) {
   667	    if (r.ok) { out(`ok    ${r.path}  (${r.rec.reviewer} / ${r.rec.disposition})`); }
   668	    else { bad++; out(`FAIL  ${r.path}`); for (const e of r.errors) out(`        ${e}`); }
   669	  }
   670	  out('');
   671	  out(bad ? `${bad} invalid record(s).` : `All ${results.length} record(s) valid.`);
   672	  return bad ? EXIT_USAGE : EXIT_PASS;
   673	}
   674	
   675	// Gather review records from explicit --review args and from the conventional
   676	// evidence directory, so CI does not have to enumerate them.
   677	function collectReviewPaths(args) {
   678	  const explicit = args.review.slice();
   679	  const dir = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
   680	  // Only the top level of reviews/ is evidence. Subdirectories are archives —
   681	  // notably reviews/legacy-unattested/, which holds records produced before
   682	  // attestation existed. Those are history and must not be able to approve or
   683	  // block anything; signing them retroactively would be the builder minting
   684	  // approvals for reviews it did not produce.
   685	  const fromDir = fs.existsSync(dir)
   686	    ? fs.readdirSync(dir, { withFileTypes: true })
   687	        .filter((d) => d.isFile() && d.name.endsWith('.json'))
   688	        .map((d) => path.join(dir, d.name))
   689	    : [];
   690	  return [...new Set([...explicit, ...fromDir])];
   691	}
   692	
   693	// ── COMMAND: --gate-done ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   694	// The fail-closed question: given what actually exists on disk, has this change
   695	// earned the right to move forward?
   696	//
   697	// Terminal states are deliberately NOT "DONE". Reviews existing is not the same
   698	// as software being correct, and a word that implies finished invites everyone
   699	// downstream to stop checking.
   700	const STATE_READY_DET = 'READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION';
   701	const STATE_READY_PR = 'READY_FOR_PR';
   702	
   703	function gateDone(args) {
   704	  const problems = [];
   705	  const observed = [];
   706	
   707	  // 1. Subject binding is MANDATORY and always enforced. Without it there is
   708	  //    no answer to "reviewed WHAT?", and every downstream check is decoration.
   709	  const subject = computeSubject({ base: args.base, head: args.head, changed: args.changed });
   710	  const claimed = args.subjectSha || args.diffSha || null;
  1070	    }
  1071	    out(`  ${step++}. Gate it:`);
  1072	    out(`     node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --gate-done --packet ${pkt} \\`);
  1073	    out(`       --subject-sha ${sha}${refs} --run-checks`);
  1074	  }
  1075	  out('');
  1076	  out('Reviewer availability on this machine:');
  1077	  out('  node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --doctor');
  1078	  return EXIT_PASS;
  1079	}
  1080	
  1081	// ── dispatch ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1082	const args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));
  1083	let code;
  1084	
  1085	// Every command runs inside this guard. A PolicyError means a rule could not be
  1086	// evaluated safely — a missing protected-path policy, an unusable git ref. That
  1087	// is a HARD BLOCK (exit 3), never a warning and never a default, because the
  1088	// alternative is proceeding while blind to what is protected.
  1089	try {
  1090	  if (args.start) code = cmdStart(args);
  1091	  else if (args.subject) code = cmdSubject(args);
  1092	  else if (args.classify) code = cmdClassify(args);
  1093	  else if (args['spec-check']) code = cmdSpecCheck(args);
  1094	  else if (args['validate-review']) code = cmdValidateReview(args);
  1095	  else if (args['gate-done']) code = cmdGateDone(args);
  1096	  else {
  1097	    process.stderr.write(`
  1098	engineering-os.cjs — deterministic half of the AI Engineering OS
  1099	
  1100	  --subject [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--changed <p> ...] [--json]
  1101	        Compute the SUBJECT of this change: the changed paths minus review
  1102	        evidence, and the sha256 every review must bind to.
  1103	
  1104	  --classify [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--changed <p> ...] [--diff-lines N]
  1105	             [--milestone] [--novel] [--json]
  1106	        Which lane, who must review. Classifies the subject. Fails toward FULL.
  1107	
  1108	  --spec-check --packet <packet.json> [--json]
  1109	        Is the intent pinned? Exit 3 if a source is neither pinnable nor
  1110	        explicitly marked "UNVERIFIED:".
  1111	
  1112	  --validate-review <review.json> [...] [--json]
  1113	        Is this record usable evidence?
  1114	
  1115	  --gate-done --subject-sha <sha> [--packet <p>] [--review <r> ...]
  1116	              [--base <ref>] [--head <ref>] [--changed <p> ...]
  1117	              [--run-checks] [--milestone] [--novel] [--json]
  1118	        Fail-closed gate. --subject-sha is MANDATORY. Exit 3 on any unmet rule.
  1119	        Reaches READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION, or READY_FOR_PR when
  1120	        --run-checks actually executed the packet's checks here.
  1121	
  1122	Exit: 0 pass · 2 usage/malformed · 3 hard-block
  1123	`);
  1124	    code = EXIT_USAGE;
  1125	  }
  1126	} catch (e) {
  1127	  if (e instanceof PolicyError) {
  1128	    process.stderr.write(`
  1129	ENGINEERING-OS HARD-BLOCK
  1130	  rule:   ENGOS-POLICY-UNAVAILABLE
  1131	  reason: ${e.message}
  1132	
  1133	Nothing was evaluated. This fails closed on purpose: a rule that cannot be
  1134	read is not a rule that passes.
  1135	`);
  1136	    code = EXIT_BLOCK;
  1137	  } else {
  1138	    throw e;
  1139	  }
  1140	}
  1141	
  1142	if (args.ledger && (args['gate-done'] || args.classify)) {
  1143	  const stamp = new Date().toISOString();
  1144	  appendLedger({
  1145	    entryId: `LED-ENGOS-${stamp.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}`,
  1146	    ts: stamp,
  1147	    agentId: 'claude-code',
  1148	    gate: 'engineering-os',
  1149	    status: code === EXIT_PASS ? 'PASS' : 'BLOCKED',
  1150	    notes: `engineering-os exit ${code}`,
  1151	  });
  1152	}
  1153	
  1154	process.exit(code);
   145	  "disposition": "APPROVE" | "APPROVE_WITH_NOTES" | "REJECT",
   146	  "findings": [
   147	    {
   148	      "severity": "CRITICAL"|"HIGH"|"MEDIUM"|"LOW"|"INFORMATIONAL",
   149	      "file": "<repo-relative path>",
   150	      "location": "<line/range/symbol>",
   151	      "problem": "<what is wrong>",
   152	      "evidence": "<the code, output, or spec line that shows it — REQUIRED, non-empty>",
   153	      "impact": "<what breaks — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   154	      "requiredCorrection": "<what must change — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   155	      "verificationMethod": "<what proves the fix — REQUIRED for CRITICAL/HIGH>",
   156	      "status": "OPEN"
   157	    }
   158	  ],
   159	  "unverified": ["<anything you could not check>"]
   160	}
   161	Rules: a finding with no evidence is an opinion — omit it. Do not manufacture
   162	findings to justify the review; zero findings is a valid, useful result.`;
   163	
   164	// CONFIRMED FINDING #6: prompts carried only the objective, paths and diff.
   165	// The packet requires reviewers to receive authoritative paths, test evidence
   166	// and explicit unverified items — without them a reviewer cannot tell an
   167	// intentional design from an accident, and cannot know which claims were
   168	// actually checked. Bounded: digests and a tail, never whole files.
   169	function evidenceBlock(ctx) {
   170	  if (!ctx) return '';
   171	  const lines = [];
   172	  if (ctx.specs && ctx.specs.length) {
   173	    lines.push('', 'PINNED SPECIFICATIONS (authoritative intent, by content digest):');
   174	    for (const sp of ctx.specs) lines.push(`  ${sp.path}  sha256:${sp.sha.slice(0, 16)}…`);
   175	  }
   176	  if (ctx.checks && ctx.checks.length) {
   177	    lines.push('', 'DETERMINISTIC EVIDENCE (already executed against this subject):');
   178	    for (const c of ctx.checks) lines.push(`  exit ${c.exit}  ${c.cmd}`);
   179	    lines.push('', 'Treat a passing check as evidence that the command ran, NOT that the');
   180	    lines.push('requirement is met. If a test passes but a user would still see wrong');
   181	    lines.push('behaviour, that is exactly the finding worth reporting.');
   182	  }
   183	  if (ctx.unverified && ctx.unverified.length) {
   184	    lines.push('', 'EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED (nobody checked these — do not assume either way):');
   185	    for (const u of ctx.unverified) lines.push(`  - ${u}`);
   186	  }
   187	  return lines.join('\n');
   188	}
   189	
   190	function codexPrompt(objective, subject, diff, ctx) {
   191	  return `You are an independent senior technical lead reviewing a change.
   192	
   193	Do not assume the implementation is correct. Independently determine what
   194	correct behaviour should be from the stated objective, then inspect what was
   195	actually written.
   196	
   197	OBJECTIVE (from the approved task packet):
   198	${objective}
   199	
   200	SUBJECT PATHS (${subject.subjectPaths.length}):
   201	${subject.subjectPaths.map((p) => '  ' + p).join('\n')}
   202	
   203	Look for: architectural drift, incomplete functionality, regression, duplicated
   204	logic, state and concurrency errors, API/contract violations, auth mistakes,
   205	security problems, missing validation, edge cases, mock or hardcoded data,
   206	misleading tests, deleted functionality.
   207	
   208	${evidenceBlock(ctx)}
   209	
   210	DIFF UNDER REVIEW:
   211	${diff}
   212	${RECORD_CONTRACT}`;
   213	}
   214	
   215	function grokPrompt(objective, subject, diff, ctx) {
   238	
   239	// ── raw output -> record ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   240	// Extract the first balanced JSON object. Models wrap JSON in prose and fences
   241	// no matter how firmly the contract says not to; that is a formatting quirk,
   242	// not a reason to discard a real review.
   243	function extractJson(text) {
   244	  if (typeof text !== 'string') return null;
   245	  const start = text.indexOf('{');
   246	  if (start === -1) return null;
   247	  let depth = 0, inStr = false, esc = false;
   248	  for (let i = start; i < text.length; i++) {
   249	    const c = text[i];
   250	    if (inStr) {
   251	      if (esc) esc = false;
   252	      else if (c === '\\') esc = true;
   253	      else if (c === '"') inStr = false;
   254	      continue;
   255	    }
   256	    if (c === '"') inStr = true;
   257	    else if (c === '{') depth++;
   258	    else if (c === '}') {
   259	      depth--;
   260	      if (depth === 0) {
   261	        try { return JSON.parse(text.slice(start, i + 1)); } catch { return null; }
   262	      }
   263	    }
   264	  }
   265	  return null;
   266	}
   267	
   268	// A parsed object is only a review if it actually carries a verdict. Some CLIs
   269	// wrap their run in a JSON envelope ({text, stopReason, usage…}); extracting
   270	// that envelope must not be mistaken for extracting a review.
   271	function isUsableReview(o) {
   272	  return !!(o && typeof o === 'object' && typeof o.disposition === 'string');
   273	}
   274	
   275	// Build a record. `parsed` null/unusable => UNAVAILABLE, never APPROVE.
   276	function buildRecord({ reviewer, reviewerModel, packetId, subject, parsed, unavailableReason, ts }) {
   277	  const base = {
   278	    reviewId: `REV-${ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14)}-${reviewer}`,
   279	    ts,
   280	    reviewer,
   281	    reviewerModel,
   282	    packetId,
   283	    reviewOf: {
   284	      diffSha256: subject.subjectSha256,
   285	      changedPaths: subject.subjectPaths.slice(),
   286	    },
   287	  };
   288	  if (!parsed || typeof parsed !== 'object' || !parsed.disposition) {
   289	    return {
   290	      ...base,
   291	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   292	      unavailableReason: unavailableReason || 'the reviewer produced no parseable review record',
   293	      findings: [],
   294	    };
   295	  }
   296	  const allowed = ['APPROVE', 'APPROVE_WITH_NOTES', 'REJECT'];
   297	  if (!allowed.includes(parsed.disposition)) {
   298	    return {
   299	      ...base,
   300	      disposition: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   301	      unavailableReason: `the reviewer returned an unrecognised disposition ${JSON.stringify(parsed.disposition)}`,
   302	      findings: [],
   303	    };
   304	  }
   305	  const findings = Array.isArray(parsed.findings) ? parsed.findings : [];
   306	  return {
   307	    ...base,
   308	    disposition: parsed.disposition,
   309	    findings: findings
   310	      // A finding with empty evidence is dropped rather than passed through to
   311	      // fail schema validation and take the whole record down with it. Dropping
   312	      // an unevidenced finding is safe; dropping an evidenced one never happens.
   313	      .filter((f) => f && typeof f.evidence === 'string' && f.evidence.trim() !== '')
   314	      .map((f) => ({
   315	        severity: f.severity || 'INFORMATIONAL',
   316	        file: f.file || '(unspecified)',
   317	        location: f.location || '',
   318	        problem: f.problem || '',
   319	        evidence: f.evidence,
   320	        impact: f.impact || '',
   321	        requiredCorrection: f.requiredCorrection || '',
   322	        verificationMethod: f.verificationMethod || '',
   323	        status: 'OPEN',
   324	      })),
   325	    unverified: Array.isArray(parsed.unverified) ? parsed.unverified.map(String) : [],
   326	  };
   327	}
   328	
   329	function validateRecord(recordPath) {
   330	  const r = spawnSync('node', [ENGOS, '--validate-review', recordPath, '--json'],
   331	    { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   332	  let parsed = [];
   333	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* fall through */ }
   334	  const first = parsed[0] || { ok: false, errors: ['validator produced no result'] };
   335	  return { ok: !!first.ok, errors: first.errors || [] };
   336	}
   337	
   338	// ── run ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   399	// Build the exact argument vector a reviewer will be launched with. Exported
   400	// so a test can assert that every bound the policy DECLARES is actually
   401	// PRESENT on the command line — the gap that let maxTurns:1 run 13 turns.
   402	function buildToolArgv(reviewer, prompt, bounds) {
   403	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   404	    return ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   405	  }
   406	  const argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   407	  if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   408	  if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   409	  return argv;
   410	}
   411	
   412	function runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec, opts = {}) {
   413	  const t = TOOLS[reviewer];
   414	  if (!t) return { ok: false, reason: `unknown reviewer ${reviewer}` };
   415	  const gateResult = authorizeLaunch(reviewer, opts);
   416	  if (!gateResult.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gateResult.reason, raw: '' };
   417	  if (!isExecutable(t.bin)) {
   418	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} is not executable at ${t.bin}`, raw: '' };
   419	  }
   420	  let argv;
   421	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   422	    // read-only sandbox; the model cannot write regardless of what it decides.
   423	    argv = ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   424	  } else {
   425	    // Bounds come from MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json, not from this file, so the
   426	    // Product Owner can tighten an adversarial reviewer without editing code.
   427	    // Single-turn print mode, no approval flags, no web, no sub-agents: the
   428	    // adversary gets exactly one pass at the diff and no way to spend the
   429	    // budget on a conversation with itself.
   430	    argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   431	    let bounds = null;
   432	    try {
   433	      bounds = (require('./tool-router.cjs').loadPolicy().roles['adversarial-review'] || {}).bounds || null;
   434	    } catch { /* absent policy is handled by the router's own refusal */ }
   435	    // CONFIRMED DEFECT (2026-08-24, caught by reading a real run's telemetry):
   436	    // the policy declared maxTurns:1 and this adapter never passed it. Grok ran
   437	    // 13 turns and returned no record. A bound that is declared in a policy but
   438	    // not passed to the tool is theatre — it reads as governance in the JSON and
   439	    // enforces nothing at runtime. Every bound below is now wired to a real flag
   440	    // or refuses, and buildToolArgv() is asserted against the policy by test.
   441	    if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) {
   442	      argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   443	    }
   444	    if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   445	    // Sub-agents are disabled by OMITTING --agents entirely. Passing an empty
   446	    // value was a bug: the CLI expects a map and rejected the array outright,
   447	    // which failed the launch at argument parsing. No definitions supplied is
   448	    // the correct way to express "no sub-agents".
   449	    if (bounds && bounds.subagents === true) {
   450	      throw new Error('policy enables sub-agents for adversarial-review; this adapter deliberately does not implement that path');
   451	    }
   452	  }
   453	  const r = spawnSync(t.bin, argv, {
   454	    encoding: 'utf8',
   455	    timeout: timeoutSec * 1000,
   468	function cmdRun(args) {
   469	  const reviewer = args.reviewer;
   470	  if (!reviewer) return usage('--reviewer is required');
   471	  if (!['codex', 'grok', 'copilot'].includes(reviewer)) return usage(`unknown reviewer ${reviewer}`);
   472	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   473	  if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) return usage(`packet not found: ${args.packet}`);
   474	
   475	  const packet = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(args.packet, 'utf8'));
   476	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   477	
   478	  if (args.subjectSha && args.subjectSha !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   479	    console.error(`[review-adapters] refusing to review: --subject-sha ${args.subjectSha.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the current subject ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…`);
   480	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   481	  }
   482	
   483	  fs.mkdirSync(REVIEWS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   484	  fs.mkdirSync(RAW_DIR, { recursive: true });
   485	  const ts = new Date().toISOString();
   486	  const stamp = ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14);
   487	
   488	  // Copilot has no local binary and no read-only path that avoids mutating a
   489	  // PR. During this pass GitHub mutation is forbidden outright, so the only
   490	  // honest output is UNAVAILABLE with the reason stated.
   491	  if (reviewer === 'copilot') {
   492	    const d = detect().copilot;
   493	    const record = buildRecord({
   494	      reviewer: 'copilot',
   495	      reviewerModel: 'github-copilot-code-review',
   496	      packetId: packet.packetId,
   497	      subject,
   498	      parsed: null,
   499	      unavailableReason:
   500	        `Copilot review could not be collected. ${d.detail}. Requesting a review requires an open pull request and a GitHub API mutation, which this correction pass forbids.`,
   501	      ts,
   502	    });
   503	    return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-copilot`, '(no tool invoked — Copilot requires an open PR)');
   504	  }
   505	
   506	  const diff = subjectDiff(subject, args);
   507	  const prompt = reviewer === 'codex'
   508	    ? codexPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff)
   509	    : grokPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff);
   510	
   511	  if (args.dryRun) {
   512	    console.log(`[review-adapters] DRY RUN — ${reviewer}: would send ${prompt.length} chars over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} subject path(s). No tool invoked.`);
   513	    return EXIT_PASS;
   514	  }
   515	
   516	  const timeoutSec = Number(args.timeout) > 0 ? Number(args.timeout) : 900;
   517	  const res = runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec, {
   518	    dataClass: args.dataClass || 'INTERNAL',
   519	    allowMetered: args.allowMetered,
   520	    approvedBy: args.approvedBy,
   521	    capUsd: args.capUsd,
   522	  });
   523	  const rawPath = path.join(RAW_DIR, `${stamp}-${reviewer}.txt`);
   524	  fs.writeFileSync(rawPath, res.raw || '(no output captured)', 'utf8');
   525	
   526	  const parsed = res.ok ? extractJson(res.raw) : null;
   527	  // When a tool exits 0 but returns no usable record, "no parseable JSON" is
   528	  // true but unhelpfully vague — and a vague UNAVAILABLE reason is the kind of
   529	  // thing an operator learns to ignore. Several CLIs report why they stopped
   530	  // (cancelled, max turns, context limit); surface that instead when present,
   531	  // because "cancelled after 9 turns" and "returned prose" call for completely
   532	  // different responses.
   533	  let why = res.ok ? 'the reviewer ran but produced no parseable JSON review record' : res.reason;
   534	  if (res.ok && !isUsableReview(parsed)) {
   535	    const envelope = extractJson(res.raw);
   536	    const stop = envelope && typeof envelope.stopReason === 'string' ? envelope.stopReason : null;
   537	    const turns = envelope && typeof envelope.num_turns === 'number' ? envelope.num_turns : null;
   538	    if (stop && stop !== 'end_turn') {
   539	      why = `the reviewer stopped early (stopReason="${stop}"${turns ? `, ${turns} turns` : ''}) without emitting a review record`;
   540	    }
   541	  }
   542	  const record = buildRecord({
   543	    reviewer,
   544	    reviewerModel: reviewer === 'codex' ? 'codex-cli (ChatGPT.app)' : 'grok-cli (grok-macos-aarch64)',
   545	    packetId: packet.packetId,
   546	    subject,
   547	    parsed: isUsableReview(parsed) ? parsed : null,
   548	    unavailableReason: why,
   549	    ts,
   550	  });
   551	  return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-${reviewer}`, rawPath);
   552	}
   553	
   554	function writeRecord(record, base, rawPath) {
   555	  const outPath = path.join(REVIEWS_DIR, `${base}.json`);
   556	  fs.writeFileSync(outPath, JSON.stringify(record, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   557	  const v = validateRecord(outPath);
   558	  console.log(`[review-adapters] reviewer   : ${record.reviewer} (${record.reviewerModel})`);
   559	  console.log(`[review-adapters] disposition: ${record.disposition}`);
   560	  if (record.unavailableReason) console.log(`[review-adapters] reason     : ${record.unavailableReason}`);
   561	  console.log(`[review-adapters] findings   : ${(record.findings || []).length}`);
   562	  console.log(`[review-adapters] raw output : ${rawPath}`);
   563	  console.log(`[review-adapters] record     : ${path.relative(ROOT, outPath)}`);
   564	  if (!v.ok) {
   565	    console.error('[review-adapters] the produced record FAILED schema validation:');
   566	    for (const e of v.errors) console.error(`    ${e}`);
   567	    console.error('[review-adapters] the record is left on disk for inspection. The gate will refuse it.');
   568	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   569	  }
   570	  console.log('[review-adapters] record validates against engineering-review.schema.json');
   236	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   237	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   238	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   239	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   240	
   241	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   242	    switch (id) {
   243	      case 'spec':
   244	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   245	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   246	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   247	      case 'build':
   248	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (codex REV-…-codex, CRITICAL/HIGH set, #8): this
   249	        // returned PASS unconditionally. A diff existing is not evidence that
   250	        // it builds — nothing in a gate verdict speaks to compilation at all.
   251	        // Rendering PASS here was the projector fabricating a positive state,
   252	        // which is precisely what the amendment forbids.
   253	        if (!parsed.subject || !(parsed.subject.subjectPaths || []).length) {
   254	          return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'no subject paths — there is nothing here to build' };
   255	        }
   256	        if (/deterministic checks executed here: (\d+)\/\1 passed/.test(observed)) {
   257	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate executed the declared checks and they passed' };
   258	        }
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'a subject diff exists, but nothing in this verdict verifies it builds' };
   260	      case 'deterministic':
   261	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   262	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   263	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   264	      case 'codex':
   265	      case 'grok': {
   266	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   267	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   268	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   269	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   270	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   271	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (#8): this used to fall through to PASS after
   272	        // checking only three failure shapes. Any rule the checks above did not
   273	        // recognise — a stale binding, short coverage, ambiguous records, a
   274	        // wrong packet — silently rendered as an approval. Absence of a known
   275	        // failure is not evidence of a pass. Require the gate to have positively
   276	        // observed this reviewer's disposition instead.
   277	        if (new RegExp(`(^|\\|)\\s*${id}: (APPROVE|APPROVE_WITH_NOTES)`, 'm').test(observed)) {
   278	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate observed an approval bound to this subject' };
   279	        }
   280	        if (problems.some((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail))) {
   281	          const hit = problems.find((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail));
   282	          return { state: 'FAILED', reason: hit.rule + ': ' + String(hit.detail).slice(0, 120) };
   283	        }
   284	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no approval was observed for this reviewer on this subject' };
   285	      }
   286	      case 'copilot':
   287	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   288	      case 'gate':
   289	        return parsed.ok
   290	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   291	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   292	      case 'runtime':
   293	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   294	      case 'checkpoint':
   295	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   296	      default:
   297	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   298	    }
   299	  };
   300	
   301	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   302	}
   357	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   358	function parseArgs(argv) {
   359	  const a = {};
   360	  // CONFIRMED FINDING #8: the projector only accepted --packet and
   361	  // --subject-sha, so gateDone recomputed from `git diff HEAD` — empty once the
   362	  // reviewed change is committed. The dashboard then showed an empty subject
   363	  // for work that had actually been reviewed. The exact range the gate used is
   364	  // now forwarded rather than reconstructed from the working tree.
   365	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   366	    const t = argv[i];
   367	    if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   368	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   369	    else if (t === '--events') a.events = argv[++i];
   370	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   371	    else if (t === '--out') a.out = argv[++i];
   372	  }
   373	  return a;
   374	}

   138	      - name: Engineering OS — compute the subject once
   139	        # ONE computation of subject paths and subject hash, reused by every
   140	        # step below. Computing it twice invites two answers, and the whole
   141	        # value of a bound review is that there is exactly one thing it is
   142	        # bound to.
   143	        id: subject
   144	        run: |
   145	          set -euo pipefail
   146	          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
   147	          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
   148	             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
   149	            # CONFIRMED FINDING #2: this used `--subject` with no refs, which
   150	            # runs `git diff HEAD` — EMPTY on a clean Actions checkout. The
   151	            # empty subject was then classified LIGHT and skipped the binding
   152	            # gate entirely, so the fallback silently disabled enforcement on
   153	            # exactly the runs that needed it. HEAD's own file list is now
   154	            # enumerated explicitly, and an empty result fails closed below.
   155	            echo "Base '$BASE' unavailable — enumerating HEAD's own files."
   156	            USED_BASE=""
   157	            HEADFILES=$(git diff-tree --root --no-commit-id -r --name-only HEAD | sed '/^$/d')
   158	            if [ -z "$HEADFILES" ]; then
   159	              echo "BLOCKED: no trustworthy file range could be established for HEAD."
   160	              echo "An unknown subject is never treated as a small one."
   161	              exit 1
   162	            fi
   163	            CHANGEDARGS=()
   164	            while IFS= read -r f; do [ -n "$f" ] && CHANGEDARGS+=(--changed "$f"); done <<< "$HEADFILES"
   165	            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject "${CHANGEDARGS[@]}" --json)
   166	          else
   167	            USED_BASE="$BASE"
   168	            SUBJ=$(node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --subject --base "$BASE" --head HEAD --json)
   169	          fi
   170	          echo "$SUBJ"
   171	          SHA=$(echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>console.log(JSON.parse(s).subjectSha256))')
   172	          echo "sha=$SHA" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   173	          echo "$SUBJ" | node -e 'let s="";process.stdin.on("data",d=>s+=d).on("end",()=>{const j=JSON.parse(s);console.log(j.subjectPaths.join("\n"))})' > /tmp/subject-paths.txt
   174	          echo "count=$(wc -l < /tmp/subject-paths.txt | tr -d " ")" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   175	          # Record the EXACT base used, so the gate step certifies the same
   176	          # range rather than recomputing a different one.
   177	          echo "base=$USED_BASE" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   178	
   179	      - name: Engineering OS — bind to exactly one task packet
   180	        # A change that carries two modified packets has two competing
   181	        # authorizations and no deterministic way to choose between them.
   182	        # Rather than pick one, this blocks and says so.
   183	        id: packet
   184	        run: |
   185	          set -euo pipefail
   186	          BASE="${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || github.event.before }}"
   187	          if [ -z "$BASE" ] || [ "$BASE" = "0000000000000000000000000000000000000000" ] \
   188	             || ! git cat-file -e "${BASE}^{commit}" 2>/dev/null; then
   189	            CHANGED=$(git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-only -r HEAD)
   190	          else
   191	            CHANGED=$(git diff --name-only "$BASE" HEAD)
   192	          fi
   193	          PACKETS=$(echo "$CHANGED" | grep -E '^builder-control/packets/.*\.json$' || true)
   194	          COUNT=$(if [ -z "$PACKETS" ]; then echo 0; else echo "$PACKETS" | wc -l | tr -d ' '; fi)
   195	          echo "changed packets: $COUNT"
   196	          echo "$PACKETS"
   197	          if [ "$COUNT" -gt 1 ]; then
   198	            echo ''
   199	            echo 'BLOCKED: this change modifies more than one task packet.'
   200	            echo 'Exactly one packet authorizes a change. Two packets means two'
   201	            echo 'answers to "what was this allowed to touch", and there is no'
   202	            echo 'honest way to pick. Split the change.'
   203	            exit 1
   204	          fi
   205	          if [ "$COUNT" -eq 1 ]; then
   206	            echo "active=$PACKETS" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   207	            echo "── pinning intent for $PACKETS"
   208	            node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --spec-check --packet "$PACKETS"
   209	          else
   210	            echo "active=" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   211	            echo "No task packet added or modified by this change."
   212	          fi
   213	
   214	      - name: Engineering OS — classify the subject
   215	        id: classify
   216	        run: |
   217	          set -euo pipefail
   218	          SUBJECT_BASE="${{ steps.subject.outputs.base }}"
   219	          ARGS=()

   221	          if [ ${#ARGS[@]} -eq 0 ]; then

   223	            # how a computation failure became a governance bypass. Zero subject
   224	            # paths after a successful subject computation means the change is
   225	            # evidence-only; that is reported, and the lane is left UNKNOWN so
   226	            # the gate step still runs and decides.
   227	            echo "No subject paths — evidence-only change. Lane left UNKNOWN; the gate still runs."
   228	            echo "lane=UNKNOWN" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
   229	            exit 0
   230	          fi
   231	          # The classifier now refuses LIGHT without a line count (finding #10),
   232	          # so CI must supply the real one rather than let an unknown size pass.
   233	          if [ -n "$SUBJECT_BASE" ]; then

   237	          fi

   246	            cat /tmp/classify.txt
   247	            echo '```'
   248	          } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

   104	// Role routing: who performs a role, with the separation rules enforced.
   105	function routeRole(roleId, opts = {}) {
   106	  const policy = loadPolicy();
   107	  const role = (policy.roles || {})[roleId];
   108	  if (!role) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_ROLE', reason: `${roleId} is not a role in the routing policy.` };
   109	
   110	  const chosen = opts.model || role.default;
   111	
   112	  // A reviewer that is also the author is not a reviewer.
   113	  for (const other of role.mustDifferFrom || []) {
   114	    const otherRole = (policy.roles || {})[other];
   115	    const otherModel = (opts.assigned && opts.assigned[other]) || (otherRole && otherRole.default);
   116	    if (otherModel && otherModel === chosen) {
   117	      return {
   118	        ok: false, code: 'SELF_REVIEW_REFUSED',
   119	        reason: `${roleId} would be performed by "${chosen}", which is already performing "${other}". A model may not review its own work.`,
   120	      };
   121	    }
   122	  }
   123	
   124	  // Recursive delegation is off unless the role explicitly opts in.
   125	  const recursion = role.recursiveDelegation === true;
   126	  if (opts.wantsDelegation && !recursion) {
   127	    return {
   128	      ok: false, code: 'RECURSIVE_DELEGATION_REFUSED',
   129	      reason: `${roleId} requested sub-agent delegation, which is disabled by default. One bounded budget must not become an unbounded tree.`,
   130	    };
   131	  }
   132	
   133	  const model = (policy.models || {})[chosen];
   134	  if (!model) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_MODEL', reason: `${chosen} is not declared in the routing policy.` };
   135	
   136	  // Data sensitivity vetoes first, before capability or cost are considered.
   137	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   138	  const rank = (policy.dataClasses || {});
   139	  const want = rank[dataClass] && rank[dataClass].rank;
   140	  const max = rank[model.maxDataClass] && rank[model.maxDataClass].rank;
   141	  if (typeof want === 'number' && typeof max === 'number' && want > max) {
   142	    return {
   143	      ok: false, code: 'DATA_CLASS_REFUSED',
   144	      reason: `${dataClass} data may not be sent to ${chosen} (max ${model.maxDataClass}). Data sensitivity vetoes before capability or cost.`,
   145	    };
   146	  }
   147	
   148	  if (model.execution === 'METERED') {
   149	    const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   150	    if (!m.ok) return { ok: false, code: 'METERED_UNAUTHORIZED', reason: m.reason };
   151	  }
   152	
   153	  return {
   154	    ok: true, roleId, model: chosen, label: model.label,
   155	    execution: model.execution, dataClass,
   156	    bounds: role.bounds || null,
   157	    recursiveDelegation: recursion,
   158	    advisoryOnly: role.advisoryOnly === true,
   159	    mayApproveOwnWork: role.mayApproveOwnWork === true,
   160	  };
   161	}
   162	
   163	function route(canon, opts) {
   164	  const task = canon.taskTypes.find((t) => t.taskId === opts.task);
   165	  if (!task) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_TASK', reason: `Task ${opts.task} is not in the Tool Capability Canon.` };
   166	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   167	  if (!(dataClass in DATA_RANK)) return { ok: false, code: 'INVALID_DATA_CLASS', reason: `Unknown data class ${dataClass}.` };
   168	
   169	  const byId = new Map(canon.tools.map((t) => [t.toolId, t]));
   170	  const considered = [];
   171	  for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds) {
   172	    const tool = byId.get(toolId);
   173	    if (!tool) continue;
   174	    const reasons = [];
   175	    if (!tool.enabled || tool.availability === 'DISABLED') reasons.push('disabled');
   176	    if (tool.availability !== 'AVAILABLE') reasons.push(`availability=${tool.availability}`);
   177	    if (DATA_RANK[dataClass] > DATA_RANK[tool.maxDataClassification]) reasons.push(`data class ${dataClass} exceeds ${tool.maxDataClassification}`);
   178	    // CONFIRMED FINDING #5: a bare --allow-metered boolean used to authorize
   179	    // spending. Money now requires a name attached to the decision and a
   180	    // number attached to the spend, both bounded by the policy ceiling.
   181	    if (tool.costClass === 'METERED') {
   182	      const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   183	      if (!m.ok) reasons.push(m.reason);
   184	    }
   185	    considered.push({ toolId, eligible: reasons.length === 0, reasons });
   186	    if (reasons.length === 0) {
   187	      return {
   188	        ok: true,
   189	        taskId: task.taskId,
   190	        toolId: tool.toolId,
   191	        label: tool.label,
   192	        executionMode: tool.executionModes[0],
   193	        costClass: tool.costClass,
   194	        dataClass,
   195	        verification: tool.verification,
   196	        requiredInputs: task.requiredInputs || [],
   197	        requiredOutputs: task.requiredOutputs || [],
   198	        considered,
   199	      };
   200	    }
   201	  }
   202	  return {
   203	    ok: false,
   204	    code: 'SPECIALIST_REQUIRED',
   205	    reason: `No verified, enabled specialist can execute ${task.taskId}. Generalist fallback is ${task.generalistFallback}.`,

   936	  // 12. Deterministic checks. READY_FOR_PR is only reachable when this process
   937	  //     actually executed them — not when a caller asserts they passed.
   938	  let checks = null;
   939	  if (args.runChecks) {
   940	    if (!args.packet) {
   941	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-CHECKS-NO-PACKET', detail: '--run-checks needs a packet to read testsRequired from' });
   942	    } else {
   943	      checks = runDeterministicChecks(args.packet);
   944	      for (const c of checks) if (c.exit !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED', detail: `${c.cmd} exited ${c.exit}` });
   945	      observed.push(`deterministic checks executed here: ${checks.filter((c) => c.exit === 0).length}/${checks.length} passed`);
   946	    }
   947	  }
   948	
   949	  const ok = problems.length === 0;
   950	  const state = !ok ? 'BLOCKED' : (checks ? STATE_READY_PR : STATE_READY_DET);
   951	
   952	  return { ok, state, problems, observed, unverified, classification: cls, subject, reviewsBound: bound.length, reviewsActive: active.length, reviewsForeign: foreign.length, checks };
   953	}
   954	
   955	// Execute the packet's declared testsRequired. These are command strings from a
   956	// packet that has already passed packet-tools validation and the Builder
   957	// Control gate; they are run through `bash -lc` because that is what they are —
   958	// commands. No caller-supplied data is interpolated into them.
   959	function runDeterministicChecks(packetPath) {
   960	  let pkt;
   961	  try { pkt = readJSON(packetPath); } catch { return []; }
   962	  const cmds = (pkt.testsRequired || []).filter((c) => !/--gate-done/.test(c));
   963	  const results = [];
   964	  for (const cmd of cmds) {
   965	    const r = spawnSync('bash', ['-lc', cmd], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   966	    results.push({ cmd, exit: r.status === null ? 1 : r.status, tail: ((r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr || '')).trim().split('\n').slice(-3).join('\n') });
   967	  }
   968	  return results;
   969	}
   970	
   971	function cmdGateDone(args) {
   972	  const r = gateDone(args);
   973	  if (args.json) { out(JSON.stringify(r, null, 2)); return r.ok ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_BLOCK; }
   974	
   975	  out('ENGINEERING OS — GATE');
   976	  out('='.repeat(52));
   977	  out(`lane            : ${r.classification.lane}${r.classification.highRisk ? ' (HIGH-RISK)' : ''}`);
   978	  out(`subject         : ${r.subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}… (${r.subject.subjectPaths.length} path(s))`);
   979	  out(`required        : ${r.classification.requiredReviewers.join(' + ') || 'none — deterministic checks only'}`);
   980	  out(`review records  : ${r.reviewsActive} active / ${r.reviewsBound} bound / ${r.reviewsForeign} ignored (other subject)`);
   981	  out('');
   982	  out('OBSERVED:');
   983	  for (const o of r.observed) out(`  - ${o}`);
   984	  if (r.checks) {
   985	    out('');
   986	    out('DETERMINISTIC CHECKS (executed by this process):');
   987	    for (const c of r.checks) out(`  exit ${String(c.exit).padEnd(3)} ${c.cmd}`);
   988	  }
   989	  if (r.unverified.length) {
   990	    out('');
   991	    out('UNVERIFIED (not failures — things nobody actually checked):');
   992	    for (const u of r.unverified) out(`  - ${u}`);
   993	  }
   994	  out('');
   995	  if (!r.ok) {
   996	    out('RESULT: BLOCKED');
   997	    out('');
   998	    for (const p of r.problems) { out(`  rule   : ${p.rule}`); out(`  reason : ${p.detail}`); out(''); }
   999	    out('No override flag exists here by design. Resolve the rule, or amend the');
  1000	    out('packet authorization — which itself leaves a ledger trail.');
  1001	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
  1002	  }
  1003	  out(`RESULT: ${r.state}`);
  1004	  out('');
  1005	  if (r.state === STATE_READY_DET) {
  1015	  out('deploy verdict. Runtime validation is a separate step that has not run.');
  1016	  return EXIT_PASS;
  1017	}
  1018	// ── ledger append (reuses the ONE ledger) ───────────────────────────────────
  1019	function appendLedger(entry) {
  1020	  const tmp = path.join(require('os').tmpdir(), `engos-ledger-${process.pid}.json`);
  1021	  fs.writeFileSync(tmp, JSON.stringify(entry, null, 2));
  1022	  const r = spawnSync('node', [LEDGER_WRITER, '--append', tmp], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
  1023	  try { fs.unlinkSync(tmp); } catch { /* best effort */ }
  1024	  return r;
  1025	}
  1026	
  1027	// ── COMMAND: --start ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  1028	// The one command to run when picking up a task. It answers the three questions
  1029	// you actually have at the start — what am I changing, how much review does it
  1030	// need, and what exactly do I type next — and prints the next commands already
  1031	// filled in with this change's real subject hash. Nothing to look up, nothing
  1032	// to remember, no prompt to paste.
  1033	function cmdStart(args) {
  1034	  const subject = computeSubject({ base: args.base, head: args.head, changed: args.changed });
  1035	  const cls = classify(subject.subjectPaths, { milestone: args.milestone, novel: args.novel, diffLines: args.diffLines });
  1036	  const sha = subject.subjectSha256;
  1037	  const pkt = args.packet || '<your-packet>.json';
  1038	  const refs = (args.base ? ` --base ${args.base}` : '') + (args.head ? ` --head ${args.head}` : '');
  1039	
  1040	  if (args.json) { out(JSON.stringify({ subject, classification: cls }, null, 2)); return EXIT_PASS; }
  1041	
  1042	  out('ENGINEERING OS — START');
  1043	  out('='.repeat(60));
  1044	  out(`subject     : ${sha}`);
  1045	  out(`paths       : ${subject.subjectPaths.length}`);
  1046	  for (const p of subject.subjectPaths.slice(0, 12)) out(`              ${p}`);
  1047	  if (subject.subjectPaths.length > 12) out(`              … ${subject.subjectPaths.length - 12} more`);
  1048	  if (subject.excludedAsEvidence.length) out(`evidence    : ${subject.excludedAsEvidence.length} path(s) excluded from the subject`);
  1049	  out('');
  1050	  out(`lane        : ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? ' (HIGH-RISK)' : ''}`);
  1051	  out(`packet      : ${cls.requiresPacket ? 'REQUIRED' : 'not required'}`);
  1052	  out(`reviewers   : ${cls.requiredReviewers.join(' + ') || 'none required'}`);
  1053	  out('');
  1054	  out('NEXT:');
  1055	  if (cls.lane === 'LIGHT') {
  1056	    out('  This is the light lane. Run your deterministic checks and open the PR.');
  1057	    out('  No packet and no model review are required for a change this small.');
  1058	    out('');
  1059	    out(`  node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --gate-done --subject-sha ${sha}${refs}`);
  1060	  } else {
  1061	    let step = 1;
  1062	    if (!args.packet) {
  1063	      out(`  ${step++}. Create a task packet naming this work, then re-run --start --packet <p>.`);
  1064	      out('     node builder-control/packet-tools.cjs --new --agent claude-code --objective "…"');
  1065	    }
  1066	    for (const r of cls.requiredReviewers) {
  1067	      out(`  ${step++}. Run the ${r} review (read-only, bound to this subject):`);
  1068	      out(`     node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --run --reviewer ${r} \\`);
  1069	      out(`       --packet ${pkt} --subject-sha ${sha}${refs}`);
  1070	    }
  1071	    out(`  ${step++}. Gate it:`);
  1072	    out(`     node builder-control/engineering-os.cjs --gate-done --packet ${pkt} \\`);
  1073	    out(`       --subject-sha ${sha}${refs} --run-checks`);
  1074	  }
  1075	  out('');
  1076	  out('Reviewer availability on this machine:');
   100	function validateConfig(args, env = process.env) {
   101	  if (!Number.isInteger(args.port) || args.port < 1 || args.port > 65535) {
   102	    return { ok: false, code: 'BAD_PORT', reason: `invalid port ${args.port}` };
   103	  }
   104	
   105	  const loopback = LOOPBACK.has(args.host);
   106	  if (!loopback) {
   107	    if (!args.allowNonLoopback) {
   108	      return {
   109	        ok: false, code: 'NON_LOOPBACK_REFUSED',
   110	        reason: `refusing to bind ${args.host}. This serves internal engineering state; binding beyond loopback requires --allow-non-loopback.`,
   111	      };
   112	    }
   113	    if (!args.acknowledged) {
   114	      return {
   115	        ok: false, code: 'EXPOSURE_UNACKNOWLEDGED',
   116	        reason: 'binding beyond loopback additionally requires --i-understand-the-exposure. Two flags, because a temporary open port is rarely temporary.',
   117	      };
   118	    }
   119	  }
   120	
   121	  // Auth is mandatory everywhere, including loopback.
   122	  let token = args.token || env.AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN || null;
   123	  let generated = false;
   124	  if (!token) {
   125	    if (!loopback) {
   126	      return {
   127	        ok: false, code: 'TOKEN_REQUIRED',
   128	        reason: 'a non-loopback bind requires an explicit --token or AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN. A generated token printed to a terminal is not an access-control plan for an exposed port.',
   129	      };
   130	    }
   131	    token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('base64url');
   132	    generated = true;
   133	  }
   134	  if (token.length < 24) {
   135	    return { ok: false, code: 'WEAK_TOKEN', reason: `token is ${token.length} characters; minimum 24.` };
   136	  }
   137	
   138	  return { ok: true, config: { ...args, host: args.host, token, generated, loopback } };
   139	}
   140	
   141	function isNeverServe(p) {
   142	  return NEVER_SERVE.some((re) => re.test(p));
   143	}
   144	
   145	function handler(config) {
   146	  return (req, res) => {
   147	    const started = Date.now();
   148	    const url = new URL(req.url, `http://${config.host}:${config.port}`);
   149	    const pathname = url.pathname;
   150	
   151	    const deny = (status, msg) => {
   152	      res.writeHead(status, {
   153	        'content-type': 'text/plain; charset=utf-8',
   154	        'cache-control': 'no-store',
   155	        'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff',
   156	        'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer',
   157	      });
   158	      res.end(msg + '\n');
   159	      log(req, status, started);
   160	    };
   161	
   162	    if (req.method !== 'GET' && req.method !== 'HEAD') {
   163	      return deny(405, 'read-only host: only GET and HEAD are served');
   164	    }
   165	
   166	    // Auth first. Nothing below this line runs for an unauthenticated caller.
   167	    //
   168	    // THREE ACCEPTED CREDENTIALS, and the third is load-bearing:
   169	    // a token in the URL authenticates the DOCUMENT but not its SUBRESOURCES.
   170	    // The browser fetches state.js with no Authorization header and no query
   171	    // string, so a header-or-query-only design 401s the script tag and renders
   172	    // a blank dashboard — which is worse than an error, because the page looks
   173	    // like it loaded. On a valid token we set a derived, HttpOnly session
   174	    // cookie so subresources authenticate too. The cookie carries an HMAC of
   175	    // the token rather than the token itself, so the browser's cookie jar
   176	    // never holds the credential the operator actually typed.
   177	    const bearer = (req.headers.authorization || '').replace(/^Bearer\s+/i, '');
   178	    const queryToken = url.searchParams.get('token') || '';
   179	    const cookies = Object.fromEntries(
   180	      String(req.headers.cookie || '').split(';').map((c) => {
   181	        const i = c.indexOf('=');
   182	        return i === -1 ? [c.trim(), ''] : [c.slice(0, i).trim(), c.slice(i + 1).trim()];
   183	      }).filter(([k]) => k)
   184	    );
   185	    const sessionValue = sessionFor(config.token);
   186	    const primary = bearer || queryToken;
   187	    const authed =
   188	      (primary && timingSafeEqual(primary, config.token)) ||
   189	      (cookies.aegis_session && timingSafeEqual(cookies.aegis_session, sessionValue));
   190	
   215	    // the allow-list itself.
   216	    const file = path.resolve(DASHBOARD, entry.file);
   217	    if (!file.startsWith(DASHBOARD + path.sep)) return deny(403, 'refused: path escapes the dashboard directory');
   218	    if (!fs.existsSync(file)) {
   219	      return deny(503, `UNAVAILABLE: ${entry.file} has not been generated. Run:\n  node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js`);
   220	    }
   221	
   222	    const body = fs.readFileSync(file);
   223	    res.writeHead(200, {
   224	      ...(setCookie ? { 'set-cookie': setCookie } : {}),
   225	      'content-type': entry.type,
   226	      'content-length': body.length,
   227	      'cache-control': 'no-store',
   228	      'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff',
   229	      'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer',
   230	      'x-frame-options': 'DENY',
   231	      // The dashboard is self-contained: inline styles, no external anything.
   232	      'content-security-policy':
   233	        "default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'",
   234	    });
   235	    if (req.method === 'HEAD') res.end(); else res.end(body);
   236	    log(req, 200, started);
   237	  };
   238	}
   239	
   240	// Log the path and status, never the token or query string.
   241	function log(req, status, started) {
   242	  const p = String(req.url || '').split('?')[0];
   243	  process.stdout.write(`[aegis-host] ${status} ${req.method} ${p} ${Date.now() - started}ms\n`);
   244	}
   245	
   246	function start(args) {
   247	  const v = validateConfig(args);
   248	  if (!v.ok) {
   249	    process.stderr.write(`\nAEGIS HOSTING REFUSED\n  rule  : ${v.code}\n  reason: ${v.reason}\n\nNothing is listening.\n`);
   250	    return { ok: false, code: v.code };
   251	  }
   252	  const config = v.config;
   253	  const server = http.createServer(handler(config));
   254	  server.listen(config.port, config.host, () => {
   255	    process.stdout.write('\nAEGIS DASHBOARD — LOCAL HOST\n');
   256	    process.stdout.write('='.repeat(56) + '\n');
   257	    process.stdout.write(`bound   : http://${config.host}:${config.port}  ${config.loopback ? '(loopback only)' : '*** EXPOSED BEYOND LOOPBACK ***'}\n`);
   258	    process.stdout.write(`auth    : required${config.generated ? ' (token generated for this run)' : ' (token supplied)'}\n`);
   259	    process.stdout.write(`serves  : ${Object.keys(SERVABLE).join(', ')} — nothing else\n`);
   260	    // A SUPPLIED token is never echoed. The operator already has it, and this
   261	    // banner routinely lands in a log file, a scrollback buffer, or a CI
   262	    // artifact — which would put a live credential at rest in all three.
   263	    // A GENERATED token has to be shown once or the run is unusable, and it
   264	    // dies with the process.
   265	    if (config.generated) {
   266	      process.stdout.write(`open    : http://${config.host}:${config.port}/?token=${config.token}\n`);
   267	      process.stdout.write('          ^ generated for this run only; it is not stored and dies with this process.\n');
   268	    } else {
   269	      process.stdout.write(`open    : http://${config.host}:${config.port}/?token=<the token you supplied>\n`);
   270	      process.stdout.write('          (a supplied token is deliberately never echoed — this banner gets logged)\n');
   271	    }
   272	    process.stdout.write('\nThe repository, ledger, packets, review records and raw reviewer\n');
   273	    process.stdout.write('transcripts are NOT reachable from this process.\n');
   274	  });
   275	  return { ok: true, server, config };
